If input is\n   necessary, wait for its response before branch-specific external\n   research. Research the path selected by the user&apos;s preferences; a preference constrains\n   the research, but does not replace it. Source discovery is not source inspection. Search\n   summaries, indexes, candidate lists, and similar discovery artifacts only locate sources;\n   they do not complete research. Before a key external recommendation, inspect the\n   underlying content of an authoritative or primary source. Every material external claim\n   or decision must trace to source content inspected during this run. A source counts as\n   inspected only when its underlying content was successfully returned and non-empty. A failed,\n   redirected, not-found, empty, or discovery-only result does not qualify. If underlying content\n   is unavailable, record the evidence gap and keep the recommendation conditional. An empty\n   workspace, discovery summaries, and model memory are not sufficient evidence. Skip\n   external research when binding workspace evidence already answers the decision. Cite only\n   source content inspected during this run, keep claims within what those sources support,\n   and prefer an official primary source when sources conflict. Use roundup, comparison, or\n   tutorial pages only to discover candidates, never as the authority for a final key\n   decision; verify candidates against official documentation, standards, or project\n   repositories. Do not turn a source into a stronger claim than it makes or invent versions,\n   sizes, performance thresholds, or retry behavior.\n5. **Close delegated research.** Immediately before writing plan prose or a plan file,\n   inspect every research child you spawned. A wait call timing out is not a terminal\n   child state. While any child remains pending, check its status and keep waiting.\n   Receive every terminal result and incorporate relevant findings before drafting;\n   record failed, cancelled, or unavailable research as a gap. A plan-directory creation\n   or plan-file write while a research child is nonterminal is forbidden. If you cancel a\n   child, wait for terminal cancellation and record its result or evidence gap before\n   drafting.\n6. **Synthesize, then draft.** Immediately before drafting, build a private decision-evidence\n   map. Every external candidate that will appear anywhere in the plan as a choice,\n   alternative, fallback, risk, or rejection must map to successfully inspected authoritative\n   content. Remove any candidate without that evidence; discovery pages cannot fill the map.\n   Store the exact inspected URL in that map; do not reconstruct, abbreviate, or infer a\n   citation. Only then recommend the approach. Keep assumptions and unresolved questions\n   explicit, and make the Key Decisions, Work Plan, and Validation Plan agree with the user&apos;s\n   constraints and the gathered facts.\n\nStop researching when the decision map is supported well enough to plan. Use direct\ntools for bounded research; delegate only when parallel work materially improves the\nevidence. If the user explicitly asks to stop or shorten research, follow the latest\ninstruction and label the remaining gaps.\n\n## Quality Bar\n\nA plan is ready only when it is:\n\n- **Grounded**: cite the user goal, current facts, docs, nearby code, tests,\n  commands, logs, specs, issues, or PRs that shaped the plan when those sources\n  exist. Mark guesses as assumptions.\n- **Decision-complete**: name the key choices, the recommended choice, and why\n  reasonable alternatives were rejected when they matter.\n- **Purpose-fit**: choose the plan type that matches the work: implementation,\n  design, debug, migration/rollout, eval/research, or review/PR split.\n- **Executable**: turn the approach into ordered work units with clear\n  dependencies, owners or surfaces, and no unrelated cleanup.\n- **Verifiable**: each work unit has a focused validation command or real manual\n  check. Include E2E, black-box, or release-binary checks when the surface needs\n  them.\n- **Scope-safe**: state non-goals, risks, rollback, and compatibility impact\n  when they affect the implementation.\n- **Question-minimal**: include open questions only when local discovery cannot\n  answer them.\n- **Not just a task list**: explain the context, recommended approach, and\n  evidence path clearly enough that another person can critique the plan before\n  execution.\n\n## Explicit File Output\n\nDeliver the plan inline by default. Do not write a plan file unless the user\nexplicitly asks to save it or a governing workspace workflow requires a named\nplan artifact. Complexity, possible reuse, or a possible later handoff is not\npermission to write.\n\nWhen persistence is authorized:\n\n- If the user gives a path or the governing workflow names one, use that path.\n- If the workspace has an established durable plan location, follow it. Prefer,\n  in order: an active `specs/&lt;feature&gt;/plan.md` for Spec Kit work, an existing\n  docs or project plan convention such as `docs/plans/`, or a documented plan\n  directory.\n- If saving and no stronger convention exists, save to\n  `.agents/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-&lt;slug&gt;.md`.\n- Create parent directories as needed.\n- You may update an active `specs/&lt;feature&gt;/plan.md`, an existing\n  user-specified plan path, or a plan file you saved this session. Never\n  overwrite any other existing file unless the user explicitly asks. When\n  creating a new dated file and the chosen file exists, add a short numeric\n  suffix.\n- Do not use `/tmp` for the final plan. Temporary scratch is not a durable user\n  artifact.\n\n## User-Visible Delivery\n\nException: when the user asks for the plan in a file and also says what the chat reply should\ncontain (for example, just a one-line confirmation), the file is the delivery. Write the complete\nplan to that file and audit it as Conversational Handoff requires, then reply only as the user\nasked, with no handoff sentence at the start or end and no copy of the plan. The rule below that\nsaves a file only when the next reply can repeat it does not apply. A request that only asks for\nthe plan in a file, even a file meant to stand on its own, says nothing about the chat reply and\ndoes not trigger this exception: give the handoff and the complete plan inline as below.\n\nOtherwise, start the final reply with this exact sentence on one line so the next action stays\nvisible even when a long plan is collapsed:\n\nThis is a plan, not a special mode; I haven’t started implementation. Reply `go` to execute this plan, or tell me what to change.\n\nThen present the complete reviewable Markdown plan exactly once in that normal user-visible\nassistant reply. Saving or rereading a plan file is not presenting it.\n\nUse one canonical Markdown plan body. Keep it concise, but do not impose a fixed character or\ntoken limit or truncate material decisions, work phases, validation steps, risks, or open\nquestions. Compress supporting evidence into concise citations, never the material decisions,\nphases, validation, risks, or open questions. If you save a plan file, its content must be\nexactly the canonical body. Copy the canonical body verbatim into the normal assistant reply.\nChoose that body before writing the file; never put a longer or different plan in the file.\nSave only when you can reproduce the entire exact body in the next normal reply. If you cannot,\nskip the file and deliver the complete plan inline. Do not create two independently expanded\nversions of the plan. If you save the plan, put its path after the visible plan. The saved file\nis supplementary and never replaces the normal assistant reply, except under the exception above.\nWhen external research informs the plan, include a compact `## Sources` section containing only\nexact URLs from successful non-empty underlying-content results. Each material external decision\nmust cite one of those URLs.\n\n## Conversational Handoff\n\nBefore delivery:\n\n- Audit the saved plan through complete readback and correction until it is stable; do not\n  present the plan during this audit loop. Read the entire saved plan back after its final\n  write; do not use `head`, a partial range, or another truncated read, then check the user&apos;s\n  constraints, Key Decisions, Work Plan, and Validation Plan for contradictions.\n- From the exact final canonical body, build a final external-name inventory. Across every\n  section, include each named library, engine, API, platform, standard, product, or project that\n  supports a material external claim or recommendation, especially in decisions, alternatives,\n  fallbacks, risks, rejections, and Sources. A name that only restates a user constraint or a\n  binding workspace fact needs no external URL; keep its user or local evidence explicit instead.\n  Match every other name to an exact URL whose authoritative underlying content was successfully\n  returned and non-empty during this run. Search or discovery output, redirects, failures,\n  not-found results, and empty results do not qualify. If a name has no qualifying result, inspect\n  authoritative content now or remove the name and keep the affected claim conditional. Do not\n  deliver until every inventory entry is resolved.\n- Audit each material external decision against its matched source. Discovery pages may record\n  candidate provenance but never serve as final decision evidence. Research, correct, remove,\n  or keep conditional any claim that the inspected authoritative content does not support.\n- If either final audit changes the canonical body, repeat the consistency and external-name\n  audits. When a file is saved, rewrite it and read the entire saved plan again first. Deliver\n  only after one complete pass makes no changes.\n- Start the next normal assistant reply with the one-line conversational handoff above, then the\n  exact stable canonical body. For a saved plan, use the stable readback content directly; for an\n  inline-only plan, use the stable audited body. Do not regenerate, shorten, summarize, or omit\n  any part. Put the saved path, when one exists, after the complete body. Under the User-Visible\n  Delivery exception, reply only as the user asked instead.\n\nAfter delivery:\n\n- Do not use question tools for the final handoff. They remain reserved for\n  necessary pre-draft user-owned choices in Research step 3.\n- After the canonical plan and any saved path, repeat: “Reply `go` to execute this plan,\n  or tell me what to change.” Under the User-Visible Delivery exception, do not repeat it.\n- Stop after the handoff; do not start implementation in the planning turn.\n- On `go`, execute the preceding plan directly without regenerating it. On a change request,\n  revise and present the complete plan again.\n\n## Plan Shape\n\nWrite the plan so the next person can act without guessing. Use the smallest\nsubset of this shape that carries the decisions needed for execution:\n\n```markdown\n## Goal\n## Success Criteria\n## Approach\n## Steps\n## Validation Plan\n## Risks / Open Questions (only when material)\n```\n\nWrite `None` for open questions only after checking the repo for answers.\nKeep `Success Criteria` outcome-oriented: what must be true for the work to be\ndone. Keep `Validation Plan` evidence-oriented: exact focused commands, E2E or\nblack-box checks when relevant, expected evidence, and manual checks that\ncannot be automated.\n\nAdapt the sections to the plan type:\n\n- For implementation work, `Steps` should name the code surfaces, data flow,\n  compatibility impact, existing utilities to reuse, and test strategy. Add PR\n  slices only when the workspace workflow or review risk calls for them.\n- For design work, emphasize interfaces, invariants, alternatives rejected, and\n  migration or compatibility rules.\n- For debugging work, list hypotheses, observations needed, instrumentation or\n  logs to inspect, reproduction steps, and the evidence that will confirm or\n  falsify each hypothesis.\n- For rollout or migration work, include phases, gates, rollback, data safety,\n  monitoring, and user-visible impact.\n- For eval or research work, include the benchmark/question, corpus or sources,\n  comparison arms, success metrics, confounders, and reproducibility evidence.\n\nDo not invent an issue, spec, PR, or file path just to fill a section. If the\nworkspace rules require them, cite the real item or state the missing prerequisite\nas an open question or blocker. Do not repeat background the user already knows or\nadd empty sections. Keep the plan decision-complete enough that an implementer does\nnot need to invent scope, interfaces, or verification.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Classify whether planning is actually needed. If the task is simple AND the\n   skill was offered automatically, say so and answer or implement under normal\n   rules instead. On an explicit invocation, plan it regardless of size.\n2. Complete Research Before Drafting when it applies. Ground the plan in\n   available truth: user request, repo/docs/code, logs, configs, prior\n   decisions, and issue/spec/PR context when it exists.\n3. Identify the plan type and the smallest viable path that proves the approach.\n4. Choose the approach that matches existing patterns with the least new\n   abstraction or process.\n5. Split work only where sequencing, risk, ownership, review, or validation\n   needs a real boundary.\n6. Map every work unit to validation evidence or a real manual check.\n7. If the user or workflow requested a saved plan, briefly report its path. Do not\n   announce the absence of a file for the normal inline case.\n8. Highlight the highest-risk validation step.\n9. Present the plan in the User-Visible Delivery form and stop for the user&apos;s\n   ordinary next message before implementation.\n\n## Exit\n\n- After generating the plan, present the conversational handoff, the complete canonical\n  Markdown, and the repeated handoff in one normal assistant reply. Under the User-Visible\n  Delivery exception, reply only as the user asked instead.\n- Stop for the user&apos;s ordinary next message. Do not treat planning text as permission to edit.\n- If the user says go, continue, implement, or otherwise starts execution, use the\n  preceding plan directly: leave planning and do the work under the normal workspace\n  rules, carrying the plan&apos;s structural commitments (see Executing The Plan).\n- Re-check the latest user message before editing so stale plan assumptions do\n  not override a new instruction.\n\n## Executing The Plan\n\nThese rules bind during execution whether the plan came from this skill or was\nsupplied by the user, and whether or not this skill was ever invoked. If you\nloaded this skill at publish time, only this section applies — the planning\nrestrictions above do not.\n\n- A plan&apos;s structural commitments survive into execution: the diff/commit/PR\n  split and the unit ordering stay binding while the work is carried out and\n  published.\n- This governs the shape of publication, never its authorization: [redacted]\n  pushing, or publishing still needs the user&apos;s ask, per source-control\n  safety. A plan step that says &quot;commit&quot; does not by itself authorize a\n  commit.\n- When the user has you publish work the plan divides into N separate diffs,\n  commits, or PRs, publish exactly that split: commit each planned unit\n  separately, in the planned order, even when one combined commit would\n  satisfy the literal request (&quot;commit this&quot;, &quot;publish it&quot;).\n- Work already finished in one mixed working tree still gets split at publish\n  time: group the changed files by plan unit and commit unit by unit. If one\n  file&apos;s changes span units, split at the hunk level (`git add -p`) or assign\n  the file to the earliest unit and say so.\n- A later explicit user instruction about the structure supersedes the plan —\n  follow it; the deviation notice is for deviations you initiate.\n- Deviate from the planned structure — collapse, merge, reorder, or skip\n  units — only after telling the user what you are changing and why, before\n  publishing a different structure than the plan promised.\n\n</skill-body>\n</read-skill-result>"}},{"id":11,"ts":"2026-11-02T01:07:19.836Z","feed":"main","feeds":["main","tools"],"kind":"tool","turn":1,"text":"[TOOL] opts.now : Date.now();\n24|\tconst staleAfterMs =\n25|\t\ttypeof opts.staleAfterMs === \"number\" && opts.staleAfterMs > 0\n26|\t\t\t? opts.staleAfterMs\n27|\t\t\t: 60000;\n28|\tconst minDisk =\n29|\t\ttypeof opts.minDiskHeadroomMb === \"number\" && opts.minDiskHeadroomMb >= 0\n30|\t\t\t? opts.minDiskHeadroomMb\n31|\t\t\t: 100;\n32|\tif (record.status !== \"active\") return { ok: false, reason: \"NOT_ACTIVE\" };\n33|\tif (record.paused === true) return { ok: false, reason: \"PAUSED\" };\n34|\tconst seen = Date.parse(record.lastSeenAt || \"\");\n35|\tif (!Number.isFinite(seen) || now - seen > staleAfterMs) {\n36|\t\treturn { ok: false, reason: \"STALE_HEARTBEAT\" };\n37|\t}\n38|\tconst caps = record.capabilities || {};\n39|\tif (caps.runnable === false) return { ok: false, reason: \"NOT_RUNNABLE\" };\n40|\tconst protocol =\n41|\t\tcaps.versions?.protocol || record.facts?.versions?.protocol;\n42|\tif (protocol !== RUNNER_PROTOCOL) {\n43|\t\treturn { ok: false, reason: \"PROTOCOL_SKEW\" };\n44|\t}\n45|\tconst runnerApi = caps.versions?.api || record.facts?.versions?.api;\n46|\tif (!runnerApiCompatible(runnerApi).ok) {\n47|\t\treturn { ok: false, reason: \"PROTOCOL_SKEW\" };\n48|\t}\n49|\t// Only the raw string marker (taskRequirements' `{ alias: name }`) is\n50|\t// unservable here. A resolved alias object (`{ name, targetIndex }`,\n51|\t// stamped by the route after central D3 resolution) carries the\n52|\t// concrete harness/model below and matches like any direct requirement.\n53|\tif (typeof req.alias === \"string\" && req.alias) {\n54|\t\treturn { ok: false, reason: \"ALIAS_UNRESOLVED\" };\n55|\t}\n56|\tif (req.harness) {\n57|\t\tconst offered = Array.isArray(caps.harnesses) ? caps.harnesses : [];\n58|\t\tif (!offered.some((h) => h && h.id === req.harness)) {\n59|\t\t\treturn { ok: false, reason: \"NO_HARNESS\" };\n60|\t\t}\n61|\t}\n62|\tif (req.model) {\n63|\t\tconst models = Array.isArray(caps.models) ? caps.models : [];\n64|\t\tif (!models.includes(req.model)) return { ok: false, reason: \"NO_MODEL\" };\n65|\t}\n66|\tif (req.tier) {\n67|\t\tconst tiers = Array.isArray(caps.tiers) ? caps.tiers : [];\n68|\t\tif (!tiers.includes(req.tier)) return { ok: false, reason: \"TIER_CEILING\" };\n69|\t}\n70|\tif (caps.diskHeadroomMb !== null && caps.diskHeadroomMb !== undefined) {\n71|\t\tif (caps.diskHeadroomMb < minDisk) return { ok: false, reason: \"DISK_LOW\" };\n72|\t}\n73|\t// Operator-owned configuration (System Settings › Runners) refines an\n74|\t// otherwise-capable match without touching Runner-reported facts. Every state transition that moves a\n12| *   task away from \"sitting where the interruption left it\" — stage moves,\n13| *   manual resume/retry, operator abort, pausing — calls this so the marker\n14| *   cannot leak onto later, unrelated work. Fails safe: an unset marker is a\n15| *   no-op and a missing task is ignored. This is the dependency-light twin of\n16| *   `consumeResumedMarker` (taskScheduler.js), which additionally persists; it\n17| *   lives here so transition code (stageTransitions/taskControl/\n18| *   stageFailureRouting) can clear the marker without importing the scheduler\n19| *   module (which would create a cycle: taskScheduler → stageTransitions is\n20| *   not currently present, but keeping the predicate layer dependency-free is\n21| *   the safer direction).\n22| * @param {Object} task - The task (mutated in place).\n23| * @returns {boolean} Whether a marker was cleared.\n24| * @example clearResumedMarker({ resumedAfterInterruption: true, interruptedAt: \"…\" }) => true\n25| ***/\n26|export function clearResumedMarker(task) {\n27|\tif (!task) return false;\n28|\tconst hadMarker = task.resumedAfterInterruption === true || !!task.interruptedAt;\n29|\ttask.resumedAfterInterruption = false;\n30|\ttask.interruptedAt = null;\n31|\treturn hadMarker;\n32|}\n33|\n34|/***\n35| * MARK: isTerminalTaskStatus\n36| * @description Whether a task status is terminal: the engine must never\n37| *   advance, fail back, retry, or (re)dispatch it. A manual complete\n38| *   landing mid-run must stick even when the orphaned run settles later.\n39| * @example isTerminalTaskStatus('completed') => true\n40| * @example isTerminalTaskStatus('in_stage') => false\n41| * @param {string} [status] - Task status value.\n42| * @returns {boolean} True for completed/done/failed/cancelled.\n43| ***/\n44|export function isTerminalTaskStatus(status) {\n45|\tconst normalized = String(status || \"\").toLowerCase();\n46|\treturn (\n47|\t\tnormalized === \"completed\" ||\n48|\t\tnormalized === \"done\" ||\n49|\t\tnormalized === \"failed\" ||\n50|\t\tnormalized === \"cancelled\"\n51|\t);\n52|}\n53|\n54|/***\n55| * MARK: findStageByIdOrName\n56| * @description Single id-or-name stage lookup shared by every transition and\n57| *   projection path, so matching semantics cannot drift between writers and\n58| *   readers (id equality wins; name matches case-insensitively). Fails safe:\n59| *   a missing/empty ref or stage list resolves to null, never a guess.\n60| * @example findStageByIdOrName([{ id: '1', name: 'Catchup' }], 'catchup') => { id: '1', name: 'Catchup' }\n61| * @param {Array<Object>} [stages] - Pipeline stages to search.\n62| * @param {string} [ref] - Stage id or name.\n63| * @returns {Object|null} The matched stage, or null.\n64| ***/\n65|export function findStageByIdOrName(stages, ref) {\n66|\tif (ref == null || String(ref).trim() === \"\") return null;\n67|\tif (!Array.isArray(stages)) return null;\n68|\tconst needle = String(ref);\n69|\treturn (\n70|\t\tstages.find(\n71|\t\t\t(s) =>\n72|\t\t\t\tString(s?.id) === needle ||\n73|\t\t\t\t(s?.name &&\n74|\t\t\t\t\tString(s.name).toLowerCase() === needle.toLowerCase()),\n75|\t\t) || null\n76|\t);\n77|}\n78|\n79|/***\n80| * MARK: isEngineOwnerOf\n81| * @description Whether the given task currently owns the engine (holds the\n82| *   single-run lock). Ids compare as strings: persisted snapshots and live\n83| *   records can drift between string and number keys, and a strict compare\n84| *   would silently treat the owner as a stranger — leaving a stranded engine\n85| *   behind on operator moves and aborts (task 072e2cfa hardening). Fails\n86| *   safe: a missing task id or engine owner is never ownership.\n87| * @example isEngineOwnerOf({ activeTaskId: 't1' }, 't1') => true\n88| * @param {Object} [engineState] - Engine snapshot.\n89| * @param {string} [taskId] - Task id to test.\n90| * @returns {boolean} True when the task holds the engine lock.\n91| ***/\n92|export function isEngineOwnerOf(engineState, taskId) {\n93|\tif (taskId == null || engineState?.activeTaskId == null) return false;\n94|\treturn String(engineState.activeTaskId) === String(taskId);\n95|}\n96|\n97|/***\n98| * MARK: findStageIndexByIdOrName\n99| * @description Index twin of `findStageByIdOrName` for ordered-pipeline walks\n100| *   (advance/failback). Same match semantics; -1 when nothing matches.\n101| * @example findStageIndexByIdOrName([{ id: '1' }, { id: '2' }], '2') => 1\n102| * @param {Array<Object>} [stages] - Pipeline stages to search.\n103| * @param {string} [ref] - Stage id or name.\n104| * @returns {number} The matched index, or -1.\n105| ***/\n106|export function findStageIndexByIdOrName(stages, ref) {\n107|\tif (ref == null || String(ref).trim() === \"\") return -1;\n108|\tif (!Array.isArray(stages)) return -1;\n109|\tconst needle = String(ref);\n110|\treturn stages.findIndex(\n111|\t\t(s) =>\n112|\t\t\tString(s?.id) === needle ||\n113|\t\t\t(s?.name && String(s.name).toLowerCase() === needle.toLowerCase()),\n114|\t);\n115|}\n116|\n117|/***\n118| * MARK: stageElapsedSeconds\n119| * @description Measures time spent in the current stage; absent timestamps are not samples.\n120| * @example stageElapsedSeconds({ enteredStageAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z' }, Date.UTC(2026, 0, 1, 0, 1)) => 60\n121| ***/\n122|export function stageElapsedSeconds(task, now = Date.now()) {\n123|\tconst entered = task.enteredStageAt ? Date.parse(task.enteredStageAt) : NaN;\n124|\treturn Number.isFinite(entered)\n125|\t\t? Math.max(0, (now - entered) / 1000)\n126|\t\t: undefined;\n127|}\n128|\n129|/***\n130| * MARK: resolveEffectiveStageType\n131| * @description Derives the effective stage type for timeout and stall\n132| *   calculations. Legacy `deploy` is a normal `ci` stage now; the runtime kind\n133| *   is not derived from command text. `merge` keeps the merge budget.\n134| * @example resolveEffectiveStageType({ type: 'ci' }) => 'ci'\n135| * @example resolveEffectiveStageType({ type: 'merge', command: './deploy.sh --prod' }) => 'merge'\n136| * @param {Object} [stage]\n137| * @returns {string}\n138| ***/\n139|export function resolveEffectiveStageType(stage) {\n140|\tif (!stage || typeof stage !== \"object\") return \"agent\";\n141|\tconst rawType = String(stage.type || \"\").toLowerCase();\n142|\tif (rawType === \"merge\") return \"merge\";\n143|\t// Legacy alias: the removed `deploy` type reads as `ci` everywhere\n144|\t// (a deploy command is an ordinary ci stage).\n145|\tif (rawType === \"deploy\") return \"ci\";\n146|\treturn rawType || \"agent\";\n147|}\n148|\n149|/***\n150| * MARK: resolveStageTimeout\n151| * @description Resolves the effective execution timeout (ms) for a stage.\n152| *   Uses explicit per-stage override if positive, otherwise the project's\n153| *   per-type timeout when set, otherwise type-specific config defaults:\n154| *     - agent / review: config.agentTimeoutMs (default: 1,800,000 ms / 30 min)\n155| *     - ci / script: config.ciTimeoutMs (default: 600,000 ms / 10 min)\n156| *     - merge: config.mergeTimeoutMs (default: 1,800,000 ms / 30 min)\n157| *     - human / unknown: 300,000 ms safe fallback\n158| * @example resolveStageTimeout({ type: 'agent' }, 'agent', undefined, { agentTimeoutMs: 60000 }) => 60000\n159| * @param {Object} [stage] - Stage configuration object.\n160| * @param {string} [stageType] - Stage type ('agent', 'ci', 'merge', etc.).\n161| * @param {Object} [cfg=config] - Config object.\n162| * @param {Object} [project] - Project carrying optional per-type timeout overrides.\n163| * @returns {number} Wall-clock timeout in milliseconds.\n164| ***/\n165|export function resolveStageTimeout(stage, stageType, cfg = config, project) {\n166|\tif (\n167|\t\tstage?.timeout !== undefined &&\n168|\t\tstage?.timeout !== null &&\n169|\t\tstage?.timeout !== \"\"\n170|\t) {\n171|\t\tconst parsed = Number(stage.timeout);\n172|\t\tif (Number.isFinite(parsed) && parsed > 0) {\n173|\t\t\treturn parsed;\n174|\t\t}\n175|\t}\n176|\n177|\t// Raw lowercase comparison (not normalizeStageType) is deliberate here:\n178|\t// legacy aliases `review`/`script` are mapped explicitly below, while\n179|\t// `human` and unknown types keep the 300000 ms safe fallback. Routing\n180|\t// through normalizeStageType would collapse unknown → agent and change\n181|\t// the fallback contract locked by characterization tests.\n182|\tconst normalizedType = String(\n183|\t\tstageType || resolveEffectiveStageType(stage) || \"agent\",\n184|\t).toLowerCase();\n185|\tconst projectTimeout = resolveProjectTimeout(project, normalizedType);\n186|\tif (projectTimeout !== undefined) {\n187|\t\treturn projectTimeout;\n188|\t}\n189|\tif (normalizedType === \"agent\" || normalizedType === \"review\") {\n190|\t\treturn cfg?.agentTimeoutMs ?? 1800000;\n191|\t}\n192|\t// Legacy alias: the removed `deploy` type reads as `ci` (a deploy\n193|\t// command is an ordinary ci stage with the CI budget).\n194|\tif (normalizedType === \"ci\" || normalizedType === \"script\" || normalizedType === \"deploy\") {\n195|\t\treturn cfg?.ciTimeoutMs ?? 600000;\n196|\t}\n197|\tif (normalizedType === \"merge\") {\n198|\t\treturn cfg?.mergeTimeoutMs ?? 1800000;\n199|\t}\n200|\treturn 300000;\n201|}\n202|\n203|/***\n204| * MARK: resolveProjectTimeout\n205| * @description Reads a project's per-type timeout override (agentTimeoutMs,\n206| *   ciTimeoutMs, mergeTimeoutMs): review stages share the\n207| *   agent timeout and legacy script stages share the CI timeout, mirroring\n208| *   the global fallback mapping. Non-positive or non-numeric values fall\n209| *   through (undefined) so invalid input can never shorten a stage to zero.\n210| * @example resolveProjectTimeout({ ciTimeoutMs: 5000 }, 'ci') => 5000\n211| * @example resolveProjectTimeout({ agentTimeoutMs: 0 }, 'agent') => undefined\n212| * @param {Object} [project] - Project carrying optional timeout overrides.\n213| * @param {string} [normalizedType] - Lowercased stage type.\n214| * @returns {number|undefined} Positive timeout in ms, or undefined to fall through.\n215| ***/\n216|export function resolveProjectTimeout(project, normalizedType) {\n217|\tif (!project || typeof project !== \"object\") return undefined;\n218|\tconst key =\n219|\t\tnormalizedType === \"agent\" || normalizedType === \"review\"\n220|\t\t\t? \"agentTimeoutMs\"\n221|\t\t\t: normalizedType === \"ci\" || normalizedType === \"script\"\n222|\t\t\t\t? \"ciTimeoutMs\"\n223|\t\t\t\t: normalizedType === \"merge\"\n224|\t\t\t\t\t\t? \"mergeTimeoutMs\"\n225|\t\t\t\t\t\t: null;\n226|\tif (!key) return undefined;\n227|\t/* eslint-disable-next-line security/detect-object-injection -- key comes from the fixed type-to-field mapping above */\n228|\tconst raw = project[key];\n229|\tif (raw === undefined || raw === null || raw === \"\") return undefined;\n230|\tconst parsed = Number(raw);\n231|\tif (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed <= 0) return undefined;\n232|\treturn Math.floor(parsed);\n233|}\n234|\n235|/***\n236| * MARK: runnerStageTimeoutStatus\n237| * @description Wall-clock check for a Runner claim hold against its stage\n238| *   budget: a live Runner renewing its lease forever must not outrun the\n239| *   stage timeout, otherwise an agent stage with a stuck harness sits\n240| *   claimed indefinitely (the engine watchdog only covers engine-owned\n241| *   stages). Anchored on the hold's start (execution time), not stage\n242| *   entry, so queue wait never consumes the budget. Unparseable anchors\n243| *   never time out — bad data fails open, the budget still applies.\n244| * @example runnerStageTimeoutStatus({ startedAt: '2026-10-29T19:00:00.000Z', timeoutMs: 1800000, now: Date.parse('2026-10-29T19:00:00.000Z') + 1800001 }) => { timedOut: true, ... }\n245| * @param {Object} [args] - Check inputs.\n246| * @param {string|null} [args.startedAt] - Claim hold start (ISO string).\n247| * @param {number} [args.timeoutMs] - Effective stage budget in ms.\n248| * @param {number} [args.now] - Clock injection (defaults to Date.now()).\n249| * @returns {{timedOut: boolean, elapsedMs: number, timeoutMs: number}} Timeout status.\n250| ***/\n251|export function runnerStageTimeoutStatus({ startedAt, timeoutMs, now = Date.now() } = {}) {\n252|\tconst startedAtMs = Date.parse(startedAt ?? \"\");\n253|\tif (!Number.isFinite(startedAtMs)) {\n254|\t\treturn { timedOut: false, elapsedMs: 0, timeoutMs };\n255|\t}\n256|\tconst elapsedMs = Math.max(0, now - startedAtMs);\n257|\tconst timedOut =\n258|\t\tNumber.isFinite(timeoutMs) && timeoutMs > 0 && elapsedMs > timeoutMs;\n259|\treturn { timedOut, elapsedMs, timeoutMs };\n260|}\n261|\n262|/***\n263| * MARK: isAutomatedStage\n264| * @description Whether a stage type is fully scripted (no model runs): `ci`\n265| *   (absorbs legacy `script` and the removed `deploy` type) or `merge`.\n266| *   Mirrors the frontend `isAutomatedStage` predicate in\n267| *   `frontend/src/components/stageTypes.js`; normalization goes through the\n268| *   single `normalizeStageType` source of truth so legacy types classify\n269| *   identically everywhere. Unknown/empty types default to `agent`\n270| *   (fail-visible: monitor pills shown, never silently hidden). A ci stage\n271| *   whose command runs the deploy script classifies here as automated via\n272| *   `ci` — no separate arm.\n273| * @example isAutomatedStage('merge') => true\n274| * @example isAutomatedStage('agent') => false\n275| * @param {string} [type] - Stage type value.\n276| * @returns {boolean} True for ci/merge stages.\n277| ***/\n278|export function isAutomatedStage(type) {\n279|\tif (!type || typeof type !== \"string\") return false;\n280|\tconst normalized = normalizeStageType(type);\n281|\treturn normalized === \"ci\" || normalized === \"merge\";\n282|}\n283|\n284|/***\n285| * MARK: resolveStageLabel\n286| * @description Resolves the human-readable label for a stage reference (id or\n287| *   name). Matches by stage id first, then case-insensitively by stage name,\n288| *   and returns the stage's display name when found. Falls back to the\n289| *   reference itself when no stage matches (or no stages are available), and\n290| *   to 'unknown' when the reference is empty. Used so history/timeline entries\n291| *   record stage names, which stay meaningful even after stages are renamed,\n292| *   reordered, inserted, or deleted.\n293| * @example resolveStageLabel('1', [{ id: '1', name: 'Idea' }]) => 'Idea'\n294| * @example resolveStageLabel('idea', [{ id: '1', name: 'Idea' }]) => 'Idea'\n295| * @example resolveStageLabel('42', [{ id: '1', name: 'Idea' }]) => '42'\n296| * @example resolveStageLabel('', [{ id: '1', name: 'Idea' }]) => 'unknown'\n297| * @example resolveStageLabel('1', []) => '1'\n298| * @param {string} [stageRef] - Stage id or name to resolve.\n299| * @param {Array<Object>} [stages=[]] - The workflow's stages.\n300| * @returns {string} Human-readable stage label (stage name when resolvable).\n301| ***/\n302|export function resolveStageLabel(stageRef, stages = []) {\n303|\tconst ref =\n304|\t\tstageRef === undefined || stageRef === null ? \"\" : String(stageRef).trim();\n305|\tif (!ref) return \"unknown\";\n306|\tif (Array.isArray(stages)) {\n307|\t\tconst match = stages.find(\n308|\t\t\t(s) =>\n309|\t\t\t\tString(s?.id) === ref ||\n310|\t\t\t\t(s?.name && String(s.name).toLowerCase() === ref.toLowerCase()),\n311|\t\t);\n312|\t\tif (match?.name) return match.name;\n313|\t}\n314|\treturn ref;\n315|}\n316|\n317|/***\n318| * MARK: resolveStage\n319| * @description Picks the stage a task keep working.  className=\"block w-full\"\n186|                  value={stage.fallback || stage.failback || 'previous'}\n187|                  onChange={(val) => {\n188|                    onUpdateStage?.(stage.id, *   - 'agent'  -> automated execution by the AI (Bot/script stage)\n9| *   - 'human'  -> manual checkpoint requiring human approval/review\n10| *   - 'ci'     -> automated bash verification command (a deploy is a ci\n11| *                 stage whose command runs the deploy script)\n12| *   - 'merge'  -> git operations only (integrate the task branch into main)\n13| * The `failback` field describes where a task is routed if the stage fails.\n14| * A value of 'any' means the operator may route the task to any other stage.\n15| ***/\n16|export const DEFAULT_STAGES = [\n17|\t{\n18|\t\tid: \"1\",\n19|\t\tname: \"Idea\",\n20|\t\ttype: \"agent\",\n21|\t\tprompt: \"Generate creative ideas.\",\n22|\t\tpaused: false,\n23|\t\tfailback: \"start\",\n24|\t},\n25|\t{\n26|\t\tid: \"2\",\n27|\t\tname: \"Technical Elab\",\n28|\t\ttype: \"agent\",\n29|\t\tprompt: \"Elaborate on technical specs.\",\n30|\t\tpaused: false,\n31|\t\tfailback: \"previous\",\n32|\t},\n33|\t{\n34|\t\tid: \"3\",\n35|\t\tname: \"Plan\",\n36|\t\ttype: \"agent\",\n37|\t\tprompt: \"Create a detailed plan.\",\n38|\t\tpaused: false,\n39|\t\tfailback: \"previous\",\n40|\t},\n41|\t{\n42|\t\tid: \"4\",\n43|\t\tname: \"Review Plan\",\n44|\t\ttype: \"human\",\n45|\t\tprompt: \"Human review of the plan.\",\n46|\t\tpaused: false,\n47|\t\tfailback: \"previous\",\n48|\t},\n49|\t{\n50|\t\tid: \"5\",\n51|\t\tname: \"In Progress\",\n52|\t\ttype: \"agent\",\n53|\t\tprompt: \"Executing the task.\",\n54|\t\tpaused: false,\n55|\t\tfailback: \"previous\",\n56|\t},\n57|\t{\n58|\t\tid: \"6\",\n59|\t\tname: \"Deploy\",\n60|\t\ttype: \"ci\",\n61|\t\tcommand: DEFAULT_DEPLOY_COMMAND,\n62|\t\tpaused: false,\n63|\t\tfailback: \"previous\",\n64|\t},\n65|];\n66|\n67|/***\n68| * MARK: normalizeStageType\n69| * @description Normalizes stage types to the authoritative types ('agent', 'human', 'ci', 'merge').\n70| * Legacy types are mapped: 'review' -> 'human', 'script' -> 'ci',\n71| * 'deploy' -> 'ci' (the deploy pipeline stage type was removed — a stage\n72| * whose command runs the deploy script is an ordinary ci stage with no\n73| * special runtime), 'custom'/other -> 'agent'.\n74| * @example normalizeStageType('review') => 'human'\n75| * @example normalizeStageType('deploy') => 'ci'\n76| * @param {string} [type]\n77| * @returns {'agent'|'human'|'ci'|'merge'}\n78| ***/\n79|export function normalizeStageType(type) {\n80|\tif (!type || typeof type !== \"string\") return \"agent\";\n81|\tconst lower = type.trim().toLowerCase();\n82|\tif (lower === \"human\" || lower === \"review\") return \"human\";\n83|\tif (lower === \"ci\" || lower === \"script\" || lower === \"deploy\") return \"ci\";\n84|\tif (lower === \"merge\") return \"merge\";\n85|\tif (lower === \"agent\") return \"agent\";\n86|\treturn \"agent\";\n87|}\n88|\n89|/***\n90| * MARK: normalizeStageSkills\n91| * @description Normalizes a stage's optional `skills` attachment list. Unknown fields\n92| *   on each attachment pass through untouched (non-declarative tolerance); only the\n93| *   authoritative fields are coerced: `name` (trimmed), `mode` ('direct'|'option'),\n94| *   and `config` (plain object). Entries without a usable name are dropped.\n95| *   Returns `undefined` when `skills` is absent so legacy stages are untouched.\n96| * @example normalizeStageSkills([{ name: 'a', mode: 'OPTION', config: { x: 1 }, extra: true }])\n97| *   => [{ name: 'a', mode: 'option', config: { x: 1 }, extra: true }]\n98| * @param {Array|undefined} [skills]\n99| * @returns {Array<Object>|undefined}\n100| ***/\n101|export function normalizeStageSkills(skills) {\n102|\tif (skills === undefined || skills === null) return undefined;\n103|\tif (!Array.isArray(skills)) return [];\n104|\treturn skills\n105|\t\t.filter(\n106|\t\t\t(s) =>\n107|\t\t\t\ts && typeof s === \"object\" && typeof s.name === \"string\" && s.name.trim(),\n108|\t\t)\n109|\t\t.map((s) => {\n110|\t\t\tconst normalized = { ...s, name: s.name.trim() };\n111|\t\t\tif (s.mode !== undefined) {\n112|\t\t\t\tnormalized.mode =\n113|\t\t\t\t\ttypeof s.mode === \"string\" && s.mode.trim().toLowerCase() === \"option\"\n114|\t\t\t\t\t\t? \"option\"\n115|\t\t\t\t\t\t: \"direct\";\n116|\t\t\t}\n117|\t\t\tif (s.config !== undefined) {\n118|\t\t\t\tnormalized.config =\n119|\t\t\t\t\ts.config && typeof s.config === \"object\" && !Array.isArray(s.config)\n120|\t\t\t\t\t\t? s.config\n121|\t\t\t\t\t\t: {};\n122|\t\t\t}\n123|\t\t\treturn normalized;\n124|\t\t});\n125|}\n126|\n127|/***\n128| * MARK: normalizeStage\n129| * @description Normalizes a single stage object ensuring stage type is strictly 'agent', 'human', or 'ci'.\n130| *   The optional per-stage `harness` override (blank/absent means \"inherit the\n131| *   project harness\") is trimmed; a blank or non-string value clears it\n132| *   (undefined) so persistence drops the key. The id itself is NOT validated\n133| *   here: an unknown harness warns visibly at session time and degrades to the\n134| *   project harness (see resolveHarnessForProject).\n135| *   CI stages additionally carry an optional `runOnBranch` mode ('task'\n136| *   default | 'primary' | 'custom', legacy 'main' maps to 'primary') and a\n137| *   `customBranch` name (trimmed; blank clears it). Legacy spellings\n138| *   `executionBranch`/`customExecutionBranch` are migrated to the canonical\n139| *   names and dropped so storage converges on the first save.\n140| * @example normalizeStage({ id: '1', name: 'Test', type: 'script' }) => { id: '1', name: 'Test', type: 'ci' }\n141| * @example normalizeStage({ id: '1', harness: '  custom  ' }).harness => 'custom'\n142| * @param {Object} stage\n143| * @returns {Object}\n144| ***/\n145|export function normalizeStage(stage) {\n146|\tif (!stage || typeof stage !== \"object\") return stage;\n147|\t// The removed `deploy` type folds into `ci` with no special runtime: a\n148|\t// legacy deploy stage keeps whatever command it carried (or no command,\n149|\t// like any other command-less ci stage) and runs as ordinary CI. The\n150|\t// default deploy command is never stamped — an explicit command is the\n151|\t// only thing that makes a stage run the deploy script.\n152|\tconst normalized = {\n153|\t\t...stage,\n154|\t\ttype: normalizeStageType(stage.type),\n155|\t};\n156|\t// Optional per-stage skills (additive): normalise only when present so legacy\n157|\t// stage objects round-trip byte-identically.\n158|\tif (stage.skills !== undefined) {\n159|\t\tconst skills = normalizeStageSkills(stage.skills);\n160|\t\tif (skills !== undefined) normalized.skills = skills;\n161|\t\telse delete normalized.skills;\n162|\t}\n163|\t// Optional per-stage tags (additive): normalise only when present so legacy\n164|\t// stage objects round-trip byte-identically.\n165|\tif (stage.tags !== undefined) {\n166|\t\tconst tags = normalizeStageTags(stage.tags);\n167|\t\tif (tags !== undefined) normalized.tags = tags;\n168|\t\telse delete normalized.tags;\n169|\t}\n170|\t// Optional per-stage harness override (additive): same explicit-choice-only\n171|\t// rule as project.harness — blank clears it (undefined).\n172|\tif (stage.harness !== undefined) {\n173|\t\tnormalized.harness =\n174|\t\t\ttypeof stage.harness === \"string\" && stage.harness.trim()\n175|\t\t\t\t? stage.harness.trim()\n176|\t\t\t\t: undefined;\n177|\t}\n178|\t// Optional per-stage CI branch execution mode (additive):\n179|\t// 'task' (default, absent) | 'primary' (project default branch) | 'custom'.\n180|\t// The 'main' value and the executionBranch/customExecutionBranch key names\n181|\t// are legacy spellings from the same feature's first iteration — mapped\n182|\t// here so hand-edited or in-flight configs converge on the canonical\n183|\t// fields (the legacy keys are dropped, never re-saved).\n184|\tconst RUN_ON_BRANCH = new Set([\"task\", \"primary\", \"custom\"]);\n185|\tconst rawRunOnBranch =\n186|\t\tstage.runOnBranch !== undefined ? stage.runOnBranch : stage.executionBranch;\n187|\tif (rawRunOnBranch !== undefined) {\n188|\t\tconst v =\n189|\t\t\ttypeof rawRunOnBranch === \"string\" ? rawRunOnBranch.trim().toLowerCase() : \"\";\n190|\t\tconst mapped = v === \"main\" ? \"primary\" : v;\n191|\t\tnormalized.runOnBranch = RUN_ON_BRANCH.has(mapped) ? mapped : \"task\";\n192|\t}\n193|\tconst rawCustomBranch =\n194|\t\tstage.customBranch !== undefined ? stage.customBranch : stage.customExecutionBranch;\n195|\tif (rawCustomBranch !== undefined) {\n196|\t\tnormalized.customBranch =\n197|\t\t\ttypeof rawCustomBranch === \"string\" && rawCustomBranch.trim()\n198|\t\t\t\t? rawCustomBranch.trim()\n199|\t\t\t\t: undefined;\n200|\t}\n201|\tdelete normalized.executionBranch;\n202|\tdelete normalized.customExecutionBranch;\n203|\t// Retired (was DA9): every command stage runs on a Runner — the backend\n204|\t// never executes stage commands, so no stage is ever host-only. Strip\n205|\t// any legacy hostOnly stamp so previously stored stages rejoin fleet\n206|\t// dispatch on the next normalization.\n207|\tdelete normalized.hostOnly;\n208|\treturn normalized;\n209|}\n210|\n211|/***\n212| * MARK: migrateMergeDeployStages\n213| * @description Migrates composite \"Merge and deploy\" stages — a merge stage\n214| *   that carries a command — into a git-only merge stage plus a dedicated\n215| *   command CI stage inserted immediately after it, in the same list and\n216| *   order. The merge stage only performs git operations now, so the command\n217| *   moves to its own ordinary ci stage: git failures and command failures\n218| *   can never mix again. The inserted stage keeps the recorded command\n219| *   verbatim (including a deploy-script command, which runs as ordinary CI\n220| *   with normal failback) and fails back to the merge stage (\"previous\").\n221| *\n222| *   Idempotent: a merge stage without a command is left untouched, and the\n223| *   inserted deploy stage reuses the deterministic id `<mergeId>-deploy`, so\n224| *   re-running the migration (every project load/save normalizes again)\n225| *   never duplicates it. A non-deploy post-merge command is dropped from the\n226| *   merge stage without an inserted stage (deprecated: see DEPRECATIONS.md).\n227| * @example migrateMergeDeployStages([{ id: 'm', type: 'merge', command: './deploy.sh --prod' }])\n228| *   => [{ id: 'm', type: 'merge' }, { id: 'm-deploy', type: 'ci', command: './deploy.sh --prod' }]\n229| * @param {Array<Object>} [stages] - Already type-normalized stages.\n230| * @returns {Array<Object>} The migrated stage list (same order + inserted deploy stages).\n231| ***/\n232|export function migrateMergeDeployStages(stages) {\n233|\tif (!Array.isArray(stages)) return [];\n234|\tconst existingIds = new Set(\n235|\t\tstages.filter((s) => s && typeof s === \"object\").map((s) => String(s.id)),\n236|\t);\n237|\tconst out = [];\n238|\tfor (const stage of stages) {\n239|\t\tconst isMergeStage =\n240|\t\t\tstage &&\n241|\t\t\ttypeof stage === \"object\" &&\n242|\t\t\tString(stage.type || \"\").toLowerCase() === \"merge\";\n243|\t\tconst command =\n244|\t\t\tisMergeStage &&\n245|\t\t\t((typeof stage.command === \"string\" && stage.command.trim()) ||\n246|\t\t\t\t(typeof stage.script === \"string\" && stage.script.trim()))\n247|\t\t\t\t? stage.command || stage.script\n248|\t\t\t\t: \"\";\n249|\t\tif (!isMergeStage || !command) {\n250|\t\t\tout.push(stage);\n251|\t\t\tcontinue;\n252|\t\t}\n253|\t\t// Strip the command: a merge stage is git-only and ignores commands.\n254|\t\tconst mergeStage = { ...stage };\n255|\t\tdelete mergeStage.command;\n256|\t\tdelete mergeStage.script;\n257|\t\t// A name that advertised the composite (\"Merge and deploy\") must\n258|\t\t// not lie about the now git-only stage.\n259|\t\tconst name = String(mergeStage.name || \"\");\n260|\t\tif (/merge/i.test(name) && /deploy/i.test(name)) {\n261|\t\t\tmergeStage.name = \"Merge\";\n262|\t\t}\n263|\t\tout.push(mergeStage);\n264|\t\tif (!isDeployCommand(command)) continue; // dropped, deprecated\n265|\t\tconst deployStageId = `${String(mergeStage.id || \"merge\")}-deploy`;\n266|\t\tif (existingIds.has(deployStageId)) continue; // already migrated\n267|\t\tout.push({\n268|\t\t\tid: deployStageId,\n269|\t\t\tname: \"Deploy\",\n270|\t\t\ttype: \"ci\",\n271|\t\t\tcommand,\n272|\t\t\tpaused: false,\n273|\t\t\tfailback: The `timeout` fallback matters: without it the\n36| *   Automated-tests stage's configured 3600000ms never reached the runner\n37| *   and every gate longer than 10 minutes was SIGKILLed mid-run (task\n38| *   b5716327: green suites reported as \"exit code 1\").\n39| * @example resolveCiTimeoutMs({ timeout: 3600000 }, {}) => 3600000\n40| * @param {Object} [resolved] - resolved CI stage definition.\n41| * @param {Object} [project] - project record (optional `ciTimeoutMs`).\n42| * @returns {number} Effective CI timeout in milliseconds.\n43| ***/\n44|export const resolveCiTimeoutMs = (resolved, project) => {\n45|\treturn (\n46|\t\tNumber(resolved?.timeoutMs) ||\n47|\t\tNumber(resolved?.timeout) ||\n48|\t\tNumber(project?.ciTimeoutMs) ||\n49|\t\t600000\n50|\t);\n51|};\n52|\n53|/***\n54| * MARK: stampDispatchRunAttribution\n55| * @description Stamps one dispatched run's resolved choice onto the task —\n56| *   the runner-side mirror of the embedded dispatch stamp in\n57| *   `executeStage`, so timeline rows and reports attribute the model that\n58| *   was ACTUALLY matched, never a fallback. The requirement's harness/\n59| *   model name the run (model normalized through `servingModelFor`, so the\n60| *   \"default\" sentinel and non-LLM kinds never stamp); a resolved alias\n61| *   rides along with its name and target index. CI/merge requirements carry\n62| *   no harness/model, so nothing is stamped and their rows stay blank — a\n63| *   scripted stage must never show a model. Best-effort: a stamping failure\n64| *   never blocks the claim.\n65| * @example stampDispatchRunAttribution({ taskStore, project, task, stage, stages, req: { harness: 'pi', model: 'm', kind: 'agent' } })\n66| * @param {Object} args - `{ taskStore, project, task, stage, stages, req }`.\n67| * @returns {Object|null} The stamped attribution, or null when nothing ran behind the dispatch.\n68| ***/\n69|export function stampDispatchRunAttribution({ taskStore, project, task, stage, stages, req }) {\n70|\ttry {\n71|\t\tif (!task || !req) return null;\n72|\t\tconst resolvedAlias =\n73|\t\t\treq.alias && typeof req.alias === \"object\" && typeof req.alias.name === \"string\"\n74|\t\t\t\t? req.alias\n75|\t\t\t\t: null;\n76|\t\tconst attribution = buildTimelineAttribution({\n77|\t\t\tharnessId: typeof req.harness === \"string\" && req.harness.trim() ? req.harness.trim() : null,\n78|\t\t\tmodelRef: servingModelFor({ kind: req.kind, model: req.model }),\n79|\t\t\taliasName: resolvedAlias ? resolvedAlias.name : null,\n80|\t\t\ttargetIndex: resolvedAlias ? resolvedAlias.targetIndex : null,\n81|\t\t});\n82|\t\tif (!attribution) return null;\n83|\t\ttask.currentRunAttribution = {\n84|\t\t\tstageId: stage?.id ?? task.currentStageId ?? null,\n85|\t\t\t...attribution,\n86|\t\t};\n87|\t\tconst arrivalLabel =\n88|\t\t\tstage?.name || resolveStageLabel(stage?.id ?? task.currentStageId, stages || []);\n89|\t\tconst stampedRow = stampArrivalRowAttribution(task, arrivalLabel, attribution);\n90|\t\tif (stampedRow) {\n91|\t\t\ttry {\n92|\t\t\t\ttaskStore?._saveTaskMarkdown?.(project?.cwd, task);\n93|\t\t\t} catch {\n94|\t\t\t\t// Persisting the stamp is best-effort; the in-memory\n95|\t\t\t\t// attribution already drives the completion row.\n96|\t\t\t}\n97|\t\t}\n98|\t\treturn attribution;\n99|\t} catch {\n100|\t\treturn null;\n101|\t}\n102|}\n103|\n104|/***\n105| * MARK: prepareIntegrationAttempt\n106| * @description Backend-side preparation for one leased `integrate` dispatch\n107| *   (Step 4): fetches the origin (read-only — the backend never pushes),\n108| *   reads the remote task-branch and target-branch tips as the attempt's\n109| *   expected SHAs, and pins a stable `mergeAttemptId` on the task so every\n110| *   dispatch poll for this merge reuses one queue entry (idempotent\n111| *   re-enqueue) instead of queueing a duplicate merge per poll. Throws\n112| *   `INTEGRATION_UNPREPARED` when the refs cannot be read (no origin ref\n113| *   yet, fetch failure) — the task stays `in_stage` for the next poll.\n114| * @example await prepareIntegrationAttempt({ taskStore, projectStore, projectId: 'p', task }) => { targetBranch: 'main', ... }\n115| * @param {Object} args - `{ taskStore, projectStore, projectId, task }`.\n116| * @returns {Promise<Object>} `{ targetBranch, taskBranch, expectedTaskSha, expectedTargetSha, mergeAttemptId }`.\n117| ***/\n118|export const prepareIntegrationAttempt = async ({ taskStore, projectStore, projectId, task }) => {\n119|\tconst unprepared = (detail) => {\n120|\t\tconst err = new Error(`Integration attempt unprepared: ${detail}`);\n121|\t\terr.code = \"INTEGRATION_UNPREPARED\";\n122|\t\tthrow err;\n123|\t};\n124|\tconst project = projectStore?.getProject?.(projectId);\n125|\tconst cwd = project?.cwd;\n126|\tif (!cwd) unprepared(\"project has no checkout\");\n127|\tconst taskBranch = taskStore.resolveTaskBranch(task);\n128|\tlet targetBranch;\n129|\ttry {\n130|\t\ttargetBranch = await defaultBranch(cwd);\n131|\t} catch {\n132|\t\tunprepared(\"cannot resolve the target branch\");\n133|\t}\n134|\ttry {\n135|\t\t// Prune: a deleted remote task branch must vanish from the\n136|\t\t// remote-tracking refs too, or a later attempt would merge a\n137|\t\t// resurrected stale tip.\n138|\t\tawait git(cwd, [\"fetch\", \"--prune\", \"origin\"], { timeout: 60000 });\n139|\t} catch (e) {\n140|\t\tunprepared(`fetch failed (${e?.message || e})`);\n141|\t}\n142|\tconst readRef = async (ref) => {\n143|\t\ttry {\n144|\t\t\treturn (await git(cwd, [\"rev-parse\", \"--verify\", ref])).stdout.trim() || null;\n145|\t\t} catch {\n146|\t\t\treturn null;\n147|\t\t}\n148|\t};\n149|\tconst expectedTargetSha = await readRef(`origin/${targetBranch}`);\n150|\tconst expectedTaskSha = await readRef(`origin/${taskBranch}`);\n151|\tif (!expectedTargetSha) unprepared(`remote ref 'origin/${targetBranch}' does not exist`);\n152|\tif (!expectedTaskSha) unprepared(`remote ref 'origin/${taskBranch}' does not exist (task work not pushed yet)`);\n153|\t// A retry after a failed attempt gets a FRESH attempt id: the failed\n154|\t// lease settled terminal (landed/conflict/stale-head/failed) in the\n155|\t// integration queue, and re-enqueueing the same id would collide with\n156|\t// that settled entry instead of queueing a re-based merge.\n157|\tconst failedBefore = !!(task.lastError && (task.lastError.message || task.lastError.reason || task.lastError.stageId || task.lastError.stderr));\n158|\tlet mergeAttemptId = `merge-${String(task.id).slice(0, 8)}-${Date.now()}`;\n159|\tif (typeof task.mergeAttemptId === \"string\" && task.mergeAttemptId.trim() && !failedBefore) {\n160|\t\tmergeAttemptId = task.mergeAttemptId.trim();\n161|\t} else {\n162|\t\ttaskStore.updateTask(projectId, task.id, { mergeAttemptId }, cwd);\n163|\t\ttask.mergeAttemptId = mergeAttemptId;\n164|\t}\n165|\treturn { targetBranch, taskBranch, expectedTaskSha, expectedTargetSha, mergeAttemptId };\n166|};\n167|\n168|/***\n169| * MARK: registerRunnerRoutes\n170| * @description Runner-facing endpoints (ADR 0009 lifecycle steps 1–6): join\n171| *   registration, heartbeat, the operator approve/deny/revoke/pause/resume\n172| *   + list surface the Phase 2 onboarding UI drives, the atomic task-claim\n173| *   route backed by ClaimStore (200 + lease or 409 + holder), and the\n174| *   execution-report endpoints (progress, logs, complete-execution with\n175| *   engine-owned finish, interrupt, monitor). Credential\n176| *   scope is enforced per request: the claim's Runner always comes from\n177| *   the authenticated credential, never from the body (R04 wrong-scope).\n178| * @example await app.register(runnerRoutes, { deps })\n179| ***/\n180|export const registerRunnerRoutes = async (app, { deps }) => {\n181|\tconst {\n182|\t\trunnerRegistry,\n183|\t\tclaimStore,\n184|\t\tsteerStore,\n185|\t\texecutionLedger,\n186|\t\tintegrationQueue,\n187|\t\ttaskStore,\n188|\t\tprojectStore,\n189|\t\tresolveAndGuardProject,\n190|\t\teventBridge,\n191|\t} = deps;\n192|\n193|\tconst sendStoreError = (reply, err) => {\n194|\t\tconst status = err.statusCode || 500;\n195|\t\tconst body = { error: err.message || \"Internal error\" };\n196|\t\tif (err.code) body.code = err.code;\n197|\t\tif (err.holder) body.holder = err.holder;\n198|\t\treturn reply.status(status).send(body);\n199|\t};\n200|\n201|\tconst ciPayloadFor = async (projectId, task, stage) => {\n202|\t\tconst project = projectStore.getProject(projectId);\n203|\t\tconst stages = resolveStagesForTask(project, task);\n204|\t\t// A null/undefined stage (e.g. the single-assignment claim route) falls\n205|\t\t// back to the task's current stage — dropping the payload here would\n206|\t\t// silently strip kind/command/runOnBranch and the Runner would run the\n207|\t\t// command in the task worktree regardless of the stage's branch mode.\n208|\t\tconst resolved = stage || stages.find((x) => String(x?.id) === String(task?.currentStageId)) || stages[0];\n209|\t\tif (!resolved) return {};\n210|\t\tconst type = String(resolved.type || \"\").toLowerCase();\n211|\t\tif (![\"ci\", \"script\", \"deploy\", \"command\"].includes(type)) return {};\n212|\t\t// Branch mode rides every payload — including the error fallback — so a\n213|\t\t// Runner can never silently degrade a 'primary'/'custom' stage back to\n214|\t\t// the task worktree (which is exactly how publish stages used to fail:\n215|\t\t// deploy.sh refuses to run from a kb/* worktree).\n216|\t\tconst runOnBranch = typeof resolved.runOnBranch === \"string\" ? resolved.runOnBranch : \"task\";\n217|\t\tconst branchPayload = {\n218|\t\t\trunOnBranch,\n219|\t\t\t...(runOnBranch === \"custom\" && typeof resolved.customBranch === \"string\" && resolved.customBranch.trim()\n220|\t\t\t\t? { customBranch: resolved.customBranch.trim() }\n221|\t\t\t\t: {}),\n222|\t\t};\n223|\t\t// Generic lifecycle seam (mirrors runCommandStage): every command\n224|\t\t// stage announces entry the same way, whether a Runner or the\n225|\t\t// backend executes it. Installed continuity plugins may record\n226|\t\t// entry markers and contribute child-process environment here;\n227|\t\t// with no plugins this is an empty bag. Never throws: a plugin\n228|\t\t// failure must not break dispatch.\n229|\t\tlet lifecycleEnv = {};\n230|\t\ttry {\n231|\t\t\tconst lifecycle = await emitStageLifecycle(\"stage:enter\", {\n232|\t\t\t\tstore: taskStore,\n233|\t\t\t\tprojectId,\n234|\t\t\t\ttaskId: task?.id,\n235|\t\t\t\tstage: resolved,\n236|\t\t\t\tkind: \"ci\",\n237|\t\t\t\tcwd: project?.cwd || \"\",\n238|\t\t\t});\n239|\t\t\tif (lifecycle && typeof lifecycle.env === \"object\") {\n240|\t\t\t\tlifecycleEnv = lifecycle.env;\n241|\t\t\t}\n242|\t\t} catch {\n243|\t\t\tlifecycleEnv = {};\n244|\t\t}\n245|\t\ttry {\n246|\t\t\tconst ctx = buildCommandKeywordContext({ task, stage: resolved, project, projectCwd: project?.cwd || \"\" });\n247|\t\t\treturn {\n248|\t\t\t\tkind: \"ci\",\n249|\t\t\t\tcommand: expandStageCommand(resolved.command || resolved.script || \"echo 'CI stage completed'\", ctx),\n250|\t\t\t\ttimeoutMs: resolveCiTimeoutMs(resolved, project),\n251|\t\t\t\t...branchPayload,\n252|\t\t\t\tenv: lifecycleEnv,\n253|\t\t\t};\n254|\t\t} catch { return { kind: \"ci\", command: resolved.command || resolved.script || \"\", timeoutMs: 600000, ...branchPayload, env: lifecycleEnv }; }\n255|\t};\n256|\n257|\tconst authedRunner = (request) => {\n258|\t\tconst credential = [REDACTED](\n259|\t\t\trequest.headers?.[\"x-runner-credential\"] || \"\",\n260|\t\t).trim();\n261|\t\treturn runnerRegistry.authenticate(credential);\n262|\t};\n263|\n264|\tapp.post(\"/runners/register\", async (request, reply) => {\n265|\t\ttry {\n266|\t\t\tconst result = runnerRegistry.register({\n267|\t\t\t\trunnerId: request.body?.runnerId,\n268|\t\t\t\tjoinToken: request.body?.joinToken,\n269|\t\t\t\tfacts: request.body?.facts || {},\n270|\t\t\t});\n271|\t\t\treturn reply.status(result.status === \"pending\" ? 202 : 200).send(result);\n272|\t\t} catch (err) {\n273|\t\t\treturn sendStoreError(reply, err);\n274|\t\t}\n275|\t});\n276|\n277|\tapp.post(\"/runners/heartbeat\", async (request, reply) => {\n278|\t\ttry {\n279|\t\t\tconst runner = authedRunner(request);\n280|\t\t\tconst result = runnerRegistry.heartbeat({\n281|\t\t\t\trunnerId: request.body?.runnerId || runner.runnerId,\n282|\t\t\t\tcredential: [REDACTED](\n283|\t\t\t\t\trequest.headers?.[\"x-runner-credential\"] || \"\",\n284|\t\t\t\t).trim(),\n285|\t\t\t\tfacts: request.body?.facts || {},\n286|\t\t\t\t// G11 audit alert: one identity from multiple origins.\n287|\t\t\t\torigin: request.ip || null,\n288|\t\t\t});\n289|\t\t\t// D5 pull channel (ADR 0011): pending steers for this\n290|\t\t\t// Runner's live executions ride the heartbeat response, so\n291|\t\t\t// steering needs no second channel. Stale Runner, live\n292|\t\t\t// lease: the steer waits here until the TTL lapses.\n293|\t\t\tconst pendingSteers = [];\n294|\t\t\tfor (const hold of claimStore.liveHolds()) {\n295|\t\t\t\tif (hold.runnerId !== runner.runnerId) continue;\n296|\t\t\t\tfor (const steer of steerStore.pendingFor(hold.executionId)) {\n297|\t\t\t\t\tpendingSteers.push({\n298|\t\t\t\t\t\tsteerId: steer.steerId,\n299|\t\t\t\t\t\ttaskId: hold.taskId,\n300|\t\t\t\t\t\tprojectId: hold.projectId,\n301|\t\t\t\t\t\texecutionId: hold.executionId,\n302|\t\t\t\t\t\tepoch: hold.epoch,\n303|\t\t\t\t\t\ttext: steer.text,\n304|\t\t\t\t\t});\n305|\t\t\t\t}\n306|\t\t\t}\n307|\t\t\treturn { ...result, pendingSteers };\n308|\t\t} catch (err) {\n309|\t\t\treturn sendStoreError(reply, err);\n310|\t\t}\n311|\t});\n312|\n313|\tapp.get(\"/runners\", async () => {\n314|\t\treturn runnerRegistry.list();\n315|\t});\n316|\n317|\tapp.post(\"/runners/:id/approve\", reply) => {\n366|\t\t// Operator-owned Runner configuration for the System Settings\n367|\t\t// Runner screen (display name, tool filter, job-type switches,\n368|\t\t// directories, project override). No Runner credential needed:\n369|\t\t// same operator surface as approve/deny/pause/resume.\n370|\t\ttry {\n371|\t\t\treturn runnerRegistry.getConfig(request.params.id);\n372|\t\t} catch (err) {\n373|\t\t\treturn sendStoreError(reply, err);\n374|\t\t}\n375|\t});\n376|\n377|\tapp.patch(\"/runners/:id/config\", async (request, reply) => {\n378|\t\ttry {\n379|\t\t\treturn runnerRegistry.updateConfig(request.params.id, request.body || {});\n380|\t\t} catch (err) {\n381|\t\t\treturn sendStoreError(reply, err);\n382|\t\t}\n383|\t});\n384|\n385|\tapp.post(\"/runners/:id/skip\", async (request, reply) => {\n386|\t\t// Operator skip: advance the task this Runner currently holds past\n387|\t\t// its stage and release the lease, so the in-flight Runner report\n388|\t\t// lands stale (stage-moved) instead of re-advancing. Same operator\n389|\t\t// surface as pause/resume — no Runner credential needed. 404 when\n390|\t\t// the Runner is unknown or holds nothing; 409 when its task is\n391|\t\t// already on the last stage.\n392|\t\ttry {\n393|\t\t\tconst runnerId = request.params.id;\n394|\t\t\tconst record = runnerRegistry\n395|\t\t\t\t.records()\n396|\t\t\t\t.find((r) => r && r.runnerId === runnerId);\n397|\t\t\tif (!record) {\n398|\t\t\t\treturn reply\n399|\t\t\t\t\t.status(404)\n400|\t\t\t\t\t.send({ error: `Unknown Runner ${runnerId}.`, code: \"UNKNOWN_RUNNER\" });\n401|\t\t\t}\n402|\t\t\tconst hold = claimStore\n403|\t\t\t\t.liveHolds()\n404|\t\t\t\t.find((h) => h && h.runnerId === runnerId);\n405|\t\t\tif (!hold) {\n406|\t\t\t\treturn reply.status(404).send({\n407|\t\t\t\t\terror: `Runner ${runnerId} has no active task to skip.`,\n408|\t\t\t\t\tcode: \"NOTHING_TO_SKIP\",\n409|\t\t\t\t});\n410|\t\t\t}\n411|\t\t\tconst project = projectStore.getProject(hold.projectId);\n412|\t\t\tconst task = taskStore.getTask(hold.projectId, hold.taskId);\n413|\t\t\tif (!task) {\n414|\t\t\t\tclaimStore.invalidate(hold.taskId);\n415|\t\t\t\treturn reply.status(404).send({\n416|\t\t\t\t\terror: \"Held task not found.\",\n417|\t\t\t\t\tcode: \"NOTHING_TO_SKIP\",\n418|\t\t\t\t});\n419|\t\t\t}\n420|\t\t\tconst stages = Array.isArray(project?.stages) ? project.stages : [];\n421|\t\t\tconst index = stages.findIndex((s) => s && s.id === task.currentStageId);\n422|\t\t\tif (index === -1 || index >= stages.length - 1) {\n423|\t\t\t\treturn reply.status(409).send({\n424|\t\t\t\t\terror: `Task ${hold.taskId} is already on the last stage.`,\n425|\t\t\t\t\tcode: \"NOTHING_TO_SKIP\",\n426|\t\t\t\t});\n427|\t\t\t}\n428|\t\t\tconst next = stages[index + 1];\n429|\t\t\ttaskStore.updateTask(\n430|\t\t\t\thold.projectId,\n431|\t\t\t\thold.taskId,\n432|\t\t\t\t{ currentStageId: next.id },\n433|\t\t\t\tproject?.cwd,\n434|\t\t\t);\n435|\t\t\tclaimStore.invalidate(hold.taskId);\n436|\t\t\treturn {\n437|\t\t\t\tsuccess: true,\n438|\t\t\t\trunnerId,\n439|\t\t\t\ttaskId: hold.taskId,\n440|\t\t\t\tfrom: task.currentStageId,\n441|\t\t\t\tto: next.id,\n442|\t\t\t};\n443|\t\t} catch (err) {\n444|\t\t\treturn sendStoreError(reply, err);\n445|\t\t}\n446|\t});\n447|\n448|\tconst guardedTask = (request, reply) => {\n449|\t\tconst projectId = resolveAndGuardProject(\n450|\t\t\trequest.body?.projectId || request.query?.projectId,\n451|\t\t\treply,\n452|\t\t);\n453|\t\tif (!projectId) return null;\n454|\t\tconst task = taskStore.getTask(projectId, request.params.id);\n455|\t\tif (!task) {\n456|\t\t\treply.status(404).send({ error: \"Task not found\" });\n457|\t\t\treturn null;\n458|\t\t}\n459|\t\treturn { projectId, task };\n460|\t};\n461|\n462|\tapp.get(\"/runners/monitor\", async () => {\n463|\t\t// Multi-Runner UI (WP-3.4): who runs what right now, so\n464|\t\t// Settings › Runners can show live assignments.\n465|\t\t// `scheduler` exposes the cutover deferral counters so a task\n466|\t\t// sitting in_stage is diagnosable: a climbing `deferredDispatch`\n467|\t\t// with no Runner pickup means the fleet is empty or mismatched.\n468|\t\tconst liveAssignments = claimStore.liveHolds().map((hold) => {\n469|\t\t\tconst record = runnerRegistry.records().find((r) => r.runnerId === hold.runnerId) || null;\n470|\t\t\treturn {\n471|\t\t\t\trunnerId: hold.runnerId,\n472|\t\t\t\tprojectId: hold.projectId,\n473|\t\t\t\ttaskId: hold.taskId,\n474|\t\t\t\texecutionId: hold.executionId,\n475|\t\t\t\tepoch: hold.epoch,\n476|\t\t\t\tstartedAt: hold.startedAt ?? null,\n477|\t\t\t\tstageId: executionLedger.stageFor(hold.executionId),\n478|\t\t\t\tprompt: executionLedger.promptFor(hold.executionId),\n479|\t\t\t\tserving: executionLedger.servingFor(hold.executionId),\n480|\t\t\t\tlastSeenAt: record?.lastSeenAt ?? null,\n481|\t\t\t};\n482|\t\t});\n483|\t\tconst sched = taskStore.schedulerStats || {};\n484|\t\tconst scheduler = {\n485|\t\t\tticks: sched.ticks || 0,\n486|\t\t\tdispatched: sched.dispatched || 0,\n487|\t\t\tdeferredDispatch: sched.deferredDispatch || 0,\n488|\t\t\tdeferredTripped: sched.deferredTripped || 0,\n489|\t\t\tlastOutcome: sched.lastOutcome ?? null,\n490|\t\t\tlastDispatchAt: sched.lastDispatchAt ?? null,\n491|\t\t};\n492|\t\t// Step 6: surface integration-queue health (depth/lease per scope\n493|\t\t// via GET /runners/integration; global lease counters here) so a\n494|\t\t// stuck merge queue is visible before tasks pile up behind it.\n495|\t\tconst integration = integrationQueue ? integrationQueue.snapshot() : {};\n496|\t\treturn { ...executionLedger.snapshot(), liveAssignments, scheduler, integration Runners on Stages\\n\\n- Task: `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\\n- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take
