then…)","meta":{"toolName":"write_file","toolCallId":"call_01a199ccd78575dfb1b04954f4e23f01","args":"{\"content\":\"# Implementation Plan — Whitelist/Blacklist Runners on Stages\\n\\n- Task: `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\\n- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take publish stages, or a compliance scan runs only on onsite runners — without blocking a whole category.\\n- Principle: **make the change easy, then make the change.** Phases 0–2 are research and preparatory refactors that land as safe, behavior-preserving steps; Phases 3–6 are the actual feature.\\n- Survey status: `SURVEY.md` exists but has **no answers** (`\\\"answers\\\": {}`). Section 2 records each survey question with a recommended default (marked ASSUMPTION) so implementation can proceed; if the survey is later answered differently, only the flagged decision points change.\\n\\n## 1. Current architecture (grounded)\\n\\nHow a Runner gets work today:\\n\\n1. **Stages** live on the project/workflow (`project.stages`, persisted in `project.json`). Shape is normalized additively in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js` (`normalizeStage` / `normalizeStages`): new optional fields are tolerated only when present so legacy stages round-trip byte-identically. Stage editor UI is `frontend/src/components/workflow/StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (+ `StageTypeConfig.jsx`); the pause/fallback section at the bottom is the natural neighbor for the new control.\\n2. **Dispatch scan** (`POST /runners/dispatch` in `api/flow/runnerRoutes.js`): `scanClaimableTasks()` (`api/flow/dispatch/scanClaimableTasks.js`) finds candidate tasks, `taskRequirements(project, task)` (`api/flow/dispatch/taskRequirements.js`) derives `{ harness, model, tier, alias, runnableType, kind, stageId }`, then each candidate is tested with `runnerMatches(runner, req, { projectId })` (`api/flow/dispatch/runnerMatches.js`). Misses are loud reason codes aggregated into the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` response.\\n3. **Runner-side operator settings** are enforced in one shared helper, `operatorConfigVerdict(record, req, opts)` (`api/flow/dispatch/operatorConfigVerdict.js`), called from **three** paths so no claim route bypasses it: the dispatch scan (via `runnerMatches`), direct claim (`POST /tasks/:id/claim`), and merge-lease acquire (`POST /runners/integration/acquire`). This is the pattern to copy.\\n4. **Runner identity** is the `runnerId` string (`api/flow/runnerRegistry.js`). There are currently **no runner labels/tags** anywhere (no `labels` on records, facts, capabilities, or `operatorConfig`) — labels would be new infrastructure on both Runner (advertise) and backend (store/match) sides.\\n5. **Fleet visibility**: `GET /runners`, `GET /runners/monitor`, scheduler deferral counters. Runner list UI consumes `frontend/src/services/runners/index.js`.\\n\\nConsequences for the design:\\n\\n- A **stage-centric** rule (stage names which runners may take it) is the mirror image of the existing **runner-centric** `operatorConfig` (runner names which projects/tools it serves). It needs its own verdict helper but the same \\\"one enforcement point, called from every claim path\\\" discipline.\\n- The merge/integration path is the awkward one: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` currently enforces with only `{ kind: \\\"merge\\\" }` and no stage context. A stage allow/block list for merge stages requires resolving the task/stage there (or explicitly scoping merge out of v1 — see decision D6).\\n\\n## 2. Survey questions → decisions (all ASSUMPTION until SURVEY.md is answered)\\n\\n| # | Survey question | Recommended default | Why |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| q1 | Names, labels, or both? | **Names (runnerId) in v1; labels as a named follow-up** | Names need no new Runner/backend infrastructure and cover both motivating examples (one publish runner, onsite-only scan — operators know their runnerIds). Labels require advertise/store/match plumbing on both sides; scoping them into v1 roughly doubles the work. Data model should reserve a shape labels can extend (see D1). |\\n| q2 | One list + mode toggle, or two lists? | **One list + mode toggle** (`Only these runners` / `All except these runners`), empty/off = unrestricted | One list has no conflict semantics to define (two lists need an allow-vs-block precedence rule). Matches \\\"optional whitelist/blacklist\\\" in the task. |\\n| q3 | No allowed runner available? | **Keep waiting + loud surfacing, never fail the task** | Matches existing `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` behavior; failing would destroy queued work. Loudness comes from a new miss reason (e.g. `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`) in dispatch misses + monitor/scheduler visibility. Manual override stays possible via existing operator moves (skip/reassign), not a new bypass. |\\n| q4 | Stage-only, or also project/group defaults? | **Stage-only in v1** | The task asks for stage settings. Project defaults add precedence rules (stage vs project) for unclear value; note as follow-up. |\\n| q5 | Who can edit the lists? | **Anyone who can already edit stage settings** | No new permission system exists for stages; inventing one is out of scope. |\\n| q6 | Who sets runner labels? (conditional on labels) | **Admin-managed, centrally** (when labels land) | The compliance use case (\\\"must run onsite\\\") is meaningless if the runner self-declares its labels. Self-reported labels are spoofable; operator-owned labels in `operatorConfig` are enforceable. |\\n\\nIf any survey answer comes back differently, the plan still holds; only the flagged decision (D1–D6 in §4) changes.\\n\\n## 3. Approach: make the change easy, then make the change\\n\\n**Make the change easy (Phases 0–2, behavior-preserving):**\\n\\n- P0: research spikes that retire the two unknowns (embedded execution path; merge-lease stage resolution) before any code is shaped around them.\\n- P1: introduce the shared stage-runner-policy module + normalization + fixtures with **no wiring** (pure functions, fully tested, nothing calls them yet).\\n- P2: plumb `stageId`/stage context to the one claim path that lacks it (integration acquire) in a behavior-preserving way, or formally scope merge out with a recorded decision.\\n\\n**Make the change (Phases 3–6):**\\n\\n- P3: data model + normalization + persistence round-trip.\\n- P4: enforcement on all claim paths + loud miss reasons.\\n- P5: UI at the bottom of stage settings.\\n- P6: observability, docs, rollout/verification.\\n\\n## 4. Key design decisions\\n\\n- **D1 — Storage shape.** `stage.runnerPolicy = { mode: \\\"allow\\\" | \\\"block\\\", runners: [<runnerId>, …] }`, entirely optional/absent = unrestricted. Empty `runners` array = unrestricted regardless of mode (fail-open, never strand tasks on a mis-edit). Additive normalization in `normalizeStage` (trim, drop non-strings/blanks, dedupe) mirroring the `tags`/`skills` pattern. Reserve sibling key space (`labels` later) rather than overloading `runners`.\\n- **D2 — Enforcement point.** New pure helper `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(stageOrPolicy, runnerId, …)` in `api/flow/dispatch/` (sibling to `operatorConfigVerdict.js`), returning `{ ok, reason }` with loud codes (`RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `RUNNER_BLOCKED`). `taskRequirements()` attaches the current stage's policy to `req` (it already returns `stageId`); `runnerMatches()` enforces it **after** health/capability gates (stable existing reasons keep priority — a stale runner still reports `STALE_HEARTBEAT`) and alongside `operatorConfigVerdict`. Direct claim and integration-acquire routes call the same helper so no path bypasses it.\\n- **D3 — No fallback execution.** A restricted stage with no eligible runner waits (q3). No silent reroute to another runner, no auto-fail. The stuck/stall surfacing must distinguish \\\"waiting for an allowed runner\\\" from generic idleness (see P6).\\n- **D4 — Name matching.** Exact `runnerId` match (trimmed). Unknown/deleted runnerIds in a policy are kept (not auto-pruned) and shown as stale in the UI, so renaming a runner doesn't silently open a compliance gate. Renames are an operator action with a visible warning.\\n- **D5 — Human/non-runnable stages.** Policy is stored but inert for stages with `runnableType: false` (human stages never dispatch). UI may still show the control for consistency, with a note that it only affects runner-dispatched work.\\n- **D6 — Merge stages in v1.** Preferred: enforce on the merge path too (publish/merge is a motivating example). Cost: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` must resolve task→stage to get the policy. If P0/P2 shows that resolution is invasive, fallback is: v1 covers agent/review/ci; merge enforcement is a fast follow with the same helper. Record whichever is chosen.\\n\\n## 5. Work plan (ordered; big/research pieces first)\\n\\n### Phase 0 — Research spikes (no production code; timeboxed)\\n\\n- **0.1 Embedded execution audit.** Confirm whether any stage type still executes on the backend without a Runner claim (notes say every command stage is Runner-runnable since DA9 retirement; verify for agent/review via `executeStage.js` / scheduler paths). Output: a short note stating which execution paths a stage policy must cover, or proof that claim-path enforcement is sufficient.\\n- **0.2 Merge-path stage resolution spike.** Determine the cheapest correct way to get from `POST /runners/integration/acquire` (`projectId` + `targetBranch`) to the task's current stage policy. Options: (a) look up the queued task in `integrationQueue` by project+branch, (b) require `taskId` in the acquire body, (c) enforce only at dispatch time (the dispatch route already has `cand.stage`) and treat direct acquire as operator-trusted. Output: chosen option + estimate; feeds D6.\\n- **0.3 Runner inventory UX check.** Confirm `GET /runners` returns everything the stage-settings picker needs (id + displayName + status). If display names are missing for some records, the picker falls back to runnerId — no backend change.\\n\\n### Phase 1 — Make the change easy: shared policy module (no wiring)\\n\\n- **1.1** Create `api/flow/dispatch/stageRunnerPolicy.js` (pure): `normalizeStageRunnerPolicy(raw)` + `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(policy, runnerId)`. Fail-open on absent/malformed input (legacy-safe, like `operatorConfigVerdict`). Full unit tests, including: absent policy → allow; empty list → allow; allow-mode hit/miss; block-mode hit/miss; malformed shapes → allow; whitespace/dupes normalized.\\n- **1.2** Shared test fixtures: a small factory for stage objects with policies + runner records, reusable by dispatch/route tests. (Check existing `test-helpers` first; extend, don't duplicate.)\\n- **1.3** Wire **nothing** yet. This phase lands as dead-but-tested code, reviewable in isolation.\\n\\n### Phase 2 — Make the change easy: context plumbing (behavior-preserving)\\n\\n- **2.1** `taskRequirements()` attaches the stage's normalized policy to the returned `req` (e.g. `req.runnerPolicy`) alongside the existing `stageId`. No consumer reads it yet → no behavior change. Update `taskRequirements` characterization tests to assert the new field.\\n- **2.2** Per P0.2 outcome: plumb stage context into the integration-acquire path (still unenforced), or record the D6 fallback. No behavior change.\\n- **2.3** Add the new miss-reason strings to whatever centralizes reason codes / monitor copy (if such a registry exists; otherwise co-locate with the verdict helper). No behavior change.\\n\\n### Phase 3 — Data model + normalization + persistence\\n\\n- **3.1** `normalizeStage()` in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js`: additive `runnerPolicy` handling (present-only, legacy round-trip identical). Unit tests: legacy stages untouched; valid policies preserved; junk coerced per D1.\\n- **3.2** Persistence round-trip: save/load a project + workflow carrying policies (`projectStore` tests), including multi-workflow (`createWorkflow`/`updateWorkflow` paths that call `normalizeStages`).\\n- **3.3** Validation at the API boundary: stage create/update routes reject unknown `mode` values with 400 (but never reject unknown runnerIds — D4).\\n\\n### Phase 4 — Enforcement on every claim path\\n\\n- **4.1** `runnerMatches()`: enforce `req.runnerPolicy` after capability gates, before/after `operatorConfigVerdict` (order: keep operator verdict last so runner-owner settings stay the final word, or first — pick one, document it, test the combined case). New loud reasons in the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` misses map.\\n- **4.2** `POST /tasks/:id/claim`: call the verdict helper with the task's current stage policy; refuse with 409 + reason code (mirrors the existing `operatorConfigVerdict` 409 block in that route).\\n- **4.3** Merge path per D6: `POST /runners/dispatch` merge branch (has `cand.stage` — straightforward) and `POST /runners/integration/acquire` (uses P2.2 plumbing).\\n- **4.4** `selectRunnerForTask()` inherits enforcement via `runnerMatches` — add a test proving it.\\n- **4.5** Negative tests: a blocked runner polling dispatch gets `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` with the new reason (not a task); direct claim by a non-allowed runner gets 409; unrestricted stages behave exactly as before (regression sweep of existing dispatch/claim tests).\\n\\n### Phase 5 — UI: bottom of stage settings\\n\\n- **5.1** New section at the bottom of `StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (below pause/fallback): mode selector (`Off` / `Only these runners` / `All except these runners`) + runner multi-select fed by `GET /runners` (displayName with runnerId fallback; status shown; stale/unknown ids preserved and badged). Reuse `SelectDropdown` / existing multi-select patterns; follow `StageTagEditor` as the closest \\\"list editor in this drawer\\\" precedent.\\n- **5.2** Save path: `onUpdateStage(stage.id, { runnerPolicy })` round-trips through the existing stage save; \\\"Off\\\"/empty clears to absent (not an empty object) so stored stages stay clean.\\n- **5.3** Frontend tests: renders section; mode + selection persist via `onUpdateStage`; stale-id badging; empty/off clears. Headless only (see §6); no long-lived dev servers.\\n- **5.4** Copy: one-line explanation per mode + a note that empty = all runners, and (per D5) that human stages are unaffected.\\n\\n### Phase 6 — Observability, docs, rollout\\n\\n- **6.1 Observability.** New miss reasons appear in dispatch `misses`, `GET /runners/monitor` (or equivalent), and any scheduler deferral diagnostics, so \\\"waiting for an allowed runner\\\" is distinguishable from \\\"no runners at all\\\". Add/extend a monitor widget test if the dashboard renders per-reason breakdowns.\\n- **6.2 Docs.** Update: (a) user docs for stage settings (new section: what it does, examples for publish + onsite-compliance), (b) runner/operator docs (interaction with runner-side `operatorConfig` scoping — both gates apply), (c) `CHANGELOG.md` entry, (d) JSDoc on new/changed functions per repo convention. Check `site-docs` vs `docs` ownership before writing.\\n- **6.3 Rollout.** Feature is additive and default-off: no migration, no flag needed. Rollout = deploy + verify unrestricted behavior unchanged + one restricted stage end-to-end (see §6). Rollback = deploy previous build; stored policies are inert to old code (unknown field ignored) — verify that claim with an old-code read test if cheap.\\n\\n## 6. Validation plan (how we prove it works)\\n\\n### Automated (primary gate: `npm run test:unit`)\\n\\n- **Unit:** policy normalize + verdict matrix (P1.1); `normalizeStage` legacy round-trip + coercion (P3.1); `taskRequirements` carries policy (P2.1); `runnerMatches` reason priority incl. combined operator+stage gates (P4.1); `selectRunnerForTask` inheritance (P4.4).\\n- **Route/integration:** dispatch honors allow (only listed runner gets the task) and block (listed runner never does) across agent + ci stages; direct-claim 409s for refused runners; merge-path behavior per D6; `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` misses include the new codes with counts; full existing dispatch/claim/runner test files pass unmodified (regression proof that unrestricted behavior is identical).\\n- **Frontend:** drawer section tests (P5.3) via the existing headless runner; persistence round-trip through `saveStages` mocks.\\n- **Golden/characterization:** a project.json with policies survives save→load→save byte-stable (apart from intended normalization).\\n\\n### Manual / headless checks (no long-lived servers)\\n\\n- Two-runner scenario (or fixtures/mocks): restrict a CI \\\"publish\\\" stage to runner A; poll dispatch as B → `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` with `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`/`RUNNER_BLOCKED`; poll as A → assigned. Same for block mode inverted.\\n- Stage-settings UI check via the headless screenshot tool (`npm run capture:screenshot`) if visual confirmation is wanted — no `npm run dev` background servers.\\n- Monitor/scheduler surfaces show the waiting-for-allowed-runner state distinctly.\\n\\n### Documentation proof\\n\\n- Docs review checklist: stage-settings doc, runner doc, changelog entry, JSDoc examples all present and linked; `npm run docs:check`-style link checks pass if applicable.\\n\\n## 7. Risks and mitigations\\n\\n- **Stranded tasks (policy names a dead/renamed runner).** Mitigated by D3 (wait, don't fail) + D4 (stale ids visible, never auto-pruned) + observability (P6.1). No silent opening of gates.\\n- **Bypass via an unpatched claim path.** Mitigated by the single-helper discipline (D2) + a negative test per path (P4.5) + P0.1 audit.\\n- **Labels expected but not delivered.** q1 follow-up is explicit: v1 = names. If compliance reviewers insist on labels, that is a scoped Phase 7 (runner label advertise/store/match + admin UI), estimated separately — the `runnerPolicy` shape reserves room for it.\\n- **Scope creep (project defaults, permissions).** q4/q5 defaults recorded; anything beyond stage-only + existing editors is a follow-up.\\n- **Survey answered differently.** Only D-decisions change; phases stay the same shape. Re-check §2 before P3.\\n\\n## 8. Open questions\\n\\n- None blocking: every survey question has a recommended default in §2. The two genuine unknowns are retired by timeboxed spikes (P0.1, P0.2) before any production code is shaped around them.\\n\\n## 9. Success criteria\\n\\n- An operator can set, save, and clear a per-stage runner allow/block list from the bottom of stage settings.\\n- Restricted stages dispatch/claim **only** to eligible runners on **every** claim path; ineligible runners get loud, specific refusals.\\n- Unrestricted stages (including all legacy stages) behave exactly as before — proven by the existing suite passing unmodified plus new regression tests.\\n- \\\"Waiting for an allowed runner\\\" is visible in dispatch misses and monitor surfaces, distinct from fleet-down states.\\n- Docs + changelog updated; `npm run test:unit` green.\\n\\n## 10. Non-goals (v1)\\n\\n- Runner labels/tags (q1 follow-up / Phase 7 candidate).\\n- Project-level or stage-group defaults (q4 follow-up).\\n- New edit permissions (q5: existing stage editors).\\n- Manual override/bypass UI beyond existing operator moves (q3 default is wait + visibility).\\n- Auto-pruning or auto-migrating policies on runner rename/delete (D4: show stale, keep closed).\\n\",\"path\":\"docs/tasks/a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\"}","output":"wrote 18849 bytes to /home/[REDACTED]/.kainbain/runner-work/kb-a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/docs/tasks/a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md","isError":false,"source":"runner-structured","runnerId":"server2-alpha","executionId":"aa6de9b2-e724-4842-ae7d-4004fca1efac","seq":15},"fidelity":"normalized","seq":55,"blockId":"b31","tool":{"callId":"call_01a199ccd78575dfb1b04954f4e23f01","name":"write_file","args":"{\"content\":\"# Implementation Plan — Whitelist/Blacklist Runners on Stages\\n\\n- Task: `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\\n- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take publish stages, or a compliance scan runs only on onsite runners — without blocking a whole category.\\n- Principle: **make the change easy, then make the change.** Phases 0–2 are research and preparatory refactors that land as safe, behavior-preserving steps; Phases 3–6 are the actual feature.\\n- Survey status: `SURVEY.md` exists but has **no answers** (`\\\"answers\\\": {}`). Section 2 records each survey question with a recommended default (marked ASSUMPTION) so implementation can proceed; if the survey is later answered differently, only the flagged decision points change.\\n\\n## 1. Current architecture (grounded)\\n\\nHow a Runner gets work today:\\n\\n1. **Stages** live on the project/workflow (`project.stages`, persisted in `project.json`). Shape is normalized additively in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js` (`normalizeStage` / `normalizeStages`): new optional fields are tolerated only when present so legacy stages round-trip byte-identically. Stage editor UI is `frontend/src/components/workflow/StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (+ `StageTypeConfig.jsx`); the pause/fallback section at the bottom is the natural neighbor for the new control.\\n2. **Dispatch scan** (`POST /runners/dispatch` in `api/flow/runnerRoutes.js`): `scanClaimableTasks()` (`api/flow/dispatch/scanClaimableTasks.js`) finds candidate tasks, `taskRequirements(project, task)` (`api/flow/dispatch/taskRequirements.js`) derives `{ harness, model, tier, alias, runnableType, kind, stageId }`, then each candidate is tested with `runnerMatches(runner, req, { projectId })` (`api/flow/dispatch/runnerMatches.js`). Misses are loud reason codes aggregated into the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` response.\\n3. **Runner-side operator settings** are enforced in one shared helper, `operatorConfigVerdict(record, req, opts)` (`api/flow/dispatch/operatorConfigVerdict.js`), called from **three** paths so no claim route bypasses it: the dispatch scan (via `runnerMatches`), direct claim (`POST /tasks/:id/claim`), and merge-lease acquire (`POST /runners/integration/acquire`). This is the pattern to copy.\\n4. **Runner identity** is the `runnerId` string (`api/flow/runnerRegistry.js`). There are currently **no runner labels/tags** anywhere (no `labels` on records, facts, capabilities, or `operatorConfig`) — labels would be new infrastructure on both Runner (advertise) and backend (store/match) sides.\\n5. **Fleet visibility**: `GET /runners`, `GET /runners/monitor`, scheduler deferral counters. Runner list UI consumes `frontend/src/services/runners/index.js`.\\n\\nConsequences for the design:\\n\\n- A **stage-centric** rule (stage names which runners may take it) is the mirror image of the existing **runner-centric** `operatorConfig` (runner names which projects/tools it serves). It needs its own verdict helper but the same \\\"one enforcement point, called from every claim path\\\" discipline.\\n- The merge/integration path is the awkward one: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` currently enforces with only `{ kind: \\\"merge\\\" }` and no stage context. A stage allow/block list for merge stages requires resolving the task/stage there (or explicitly scoping merge out of v1 — see decision D6).\\n\\n## 2. Survey questions → decisions (all ASSUMPTION until SURVEY.md is answered)\\n\\n| # | Survey question | Recommended default | Why |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| q1 | Names, labels, or both? | **Names (runnerId) in v1; labels as a named follow-up** | Names need no new Runner/backend infrastructure and cover both motivating examples (one publish runner, onsite-only scan — operators know their runnerIds). Labels require advertise/store/match plumbing on both sides; scoping them into v1 roughly doubles the work. Data model should reserve a shape labels can extend (see D1). |\\n| q2 | One list + mode toggle, or two lists? | **One list + mode toggle** (`Only these runners` / `All except these runners`), empty/off = unrestricted | One list has no conflict semantics to define (two lists need an allow-vs-block precedence rule). Matches \\\"optional whitelist/blacklist\\\" in the task. |\\n| q3 | No allowed runner available? | **Keep waiting + loud surfacing, never fail the task** | Matches existing `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` behavior; failing would destroy queued work. Loudness comes from a new miss reason (e.g. `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`) in dispatch misses + monitor/scheduler visibility. Manual override stays possible via existing operator moves (skip/reassign), not a new bypass. |\\n| q4 | Stage-only, or also project/group defaults? | **Stage-only in v1** | The task asks for stage settings. Project defaults add precedence rules (stage vs project) for unclear value; note as follow-up. |\\n| q5 | Who can edit the lists? | **Anyone who can already edit stage settings** | No new permission system exists for stages; inventing one is out of scope. |\\n| q6 | Who sets runner labels? (conditional on labels) | **Admin-managed, centrally** (when labels land) | The compliance use case (\\\"must run onsite\\\") is meaningless if the runner self-declares its labels. Self-reported labels are spoofable; operator-owned labels in `operatorConfig` are enforceable. |\\n\\nIf any survey answer comes back differently, the plan still holds; only the flagged decision (D1–D6 in §4) changes.\\n\\n## 3. Headless only (see §6); no long-lived dev servers.\\n- **5.4** Copy: one-line explanation per mode + a note that empty = all runners, and (per D5) that human stages are unaffected.\\n\\n### Phase 6 — Observability, docs, rollout\\n\\n- **6.1 Observability.** New miss reasons appear in dispatch `misses`, `GET /runners/monitor` (or equivalent), and any scheduler deferral diagnostics, so \\\"waiting for an allowed runner\\\" is distinguishable from \\\"no runners at all\\\". Add/extend a monitor widget test if the dashboard renders per-reason breakdowns.\\n- **6.2 Docs.** Update: (a) user docs for stage settings (new section: what it does, examples for publish + onsite-compliance), (b) runner/operator docs (interaction with runner-side `operatorConfig` scoping — both gates apply), (c) `CHANGELOG.md` entry, (d) JSDoc on new/changed functions per repo convention. Check `site-docs` vs `docs` ownership before writing.\\n- **6.3 Rollout.** Feature is additive and default-off: no migration, no flag needed. Rollout = deploy + verify unrestricted behavior unchanged + one restricted stage end-to-end (see §6). Rollback = deploy previous build; stored policies are inert to old code (unknown field ignored) — verify that claim with an old-code read test if cheap.\\n\\n## 6. Validation plan (how we prove it works)\\n\\n### Automated (primary gate: `npm run test:unit`)\\n\\n- **Unit:** policy normalize + verdict matrix (P1.1); `normalizeStage` legacy round-trip + coercion (P3.1); `taskRequirements` carries policy (P2.1); `runnerMatches` reason priority incl. combined operator+stage gates (P4.1); `selectRunnerForTask` inheritance (P4.4).\\n- **Route/integration:** dispatch honors allow (only listed runner gets the task) and block (listed runner never does) across agent + ci stages; direct-claim 409s for refused runners; merge-path behavior per D6; `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` misses include the new codes with counts; full existing dispatch/claim/runner test files pass unmodified (regression proof that unrestricted behavior is identical).\\n- **Frontend:** drawer section tests (P5.3) via the existing headless runner; persistence round-trip through `saveStages` mocks.\\n- **Golden/characterization:** a project.json with policies survives save→load→save byte-stable (apart from intended normalization).\\n\\n### Manual / headless checks (no long-lived servers)\\n\\n- Two-runner scenario (or fixtures/mocks): restrict a CI \\\"publish\\\" stage to runner A; poll dispatch as B → `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` with `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`/`RUNNER_BLOCKED`; poll as A → assigned. Same for block mode inverted.\\n- Stage-settings UI check via the headless screenshot tool (`npm run capture:screenshot`) if visual confirmation is wanted — no `npm run dev` background servers.\\n- Monitor/scheduler surfaces show the waiting-for-allowed-runner state distinctly.\\n\\n### Documentation proof\\n\\n- Docs review checklist: stage-settings doc, runner doc, changelog entry, JSDoc examples all present and linked; `npm run docs:check`-style link checks pass if applicable.\\n\\n## 7. Risks and mitigations\\n\\n- **Stranded tasks (policy names a dead/renamed runner).** Mitigated by D3 (wait, don't fail) + D4 (stale ids visible, never auto-pruned) + observability (P6.1). No silent opening of gates.\\n- **Bypass via an unpatched claim path.** Mitigated by the single-helper discipline (D2) + a negative test per path (P4.5) + P0.1 audit.\\n- **Labels expected but not delivered.** q1 follow-up is explicit: v1 = names. If compliance reviewers insist on labels, that is a scoped Phase 7 (runner label advertise/store/match + admin UI), estimated separately — the `runnerPolicy` shape reserves room for it.\\n- **Scope creep (project defaults, permissions).** q4/q5 defaults recorded; anything beyond stage-only + existing editors is a follow-up.\\n- **Survey answered differently.** Only D-decisions change; phases stay the same shape. Re-check §2 before P3.\\n\\n## 8. Open questions\\n\\n- None blocking: every survey question has a recommended default in §2. Success criteria\\n\\n- An operator can set, save, and clear a per-stage runner allow/block list from the bottom of stage settings.\\n- Restricted stages dispatch/claim **only** to eligible runners on **every** claim path; ineligible runners get loud, specific refusals.\\n- Unrestricted stages (including all legacy stages) behave exactly as before — proven by the existing suite passing unmodified plus new regression tests.\\n- \\\"Waiting for an allowed runner\\\" is visible in dispatch misses and monitor surfaces, distinct from fleet-down states.\\n- Docs + changelog updated; `npm run test:unit` green.\\n\\n## 10. Non-goals (v1)\\n\\n- Runner labels/tags (q1 follow-up / Phase 7 candidate).\\n- Project-level or stage-group defaults (q4 follow-up).\\n- New edit permissions (q5: existing stage editors).\\n- Manual `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\n4|- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take publish stages, or a compliance scan runs only on onsite runners — without blocking a whole category.\n5|- Principle: **make the change easy, then make the change.** Phases 0–2 are research and preparatory refactors that land as safe, behavior-preserving steps; Phases 3–6 are the actual feature.\n6|- Survey status: `SURVEY.md` exists but has **no answers** (`\"answers\": {}`). Section 2 records each survey question with a recommended default (marked ASSUMPTION) so implementation can proceed; if the survey is later answered differently, only the flagged decision points change.\n7|\n8|## 1. Current architecture (grounded)\n9|\n10|How a Runner gets work today:\n11|\n12|1. **Stages** live on the project/workflow (`project.stages`, persisted in `project.json`). Shape is normalized additively in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js` (`normalizeStage` / `normalizeStages`): new optional fields are tolerated only when present so legacy stages round-trip byte-identically. Stage editor UI is `frontend/src/components/workflow/StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (+ `StageTypeConfig.jsx`); the pause/fallback section at the bottom is the natural neighbor for the new control.\n13|2. **Dispatch scan** (`POST /runners/dispatch` in `api/flow/runnerRoutes.js`): `scanClaimableTasks()` (`api/flow/dispatch/scanClaimableTasks.js`) finds candidate tasks, `taskRequirements(project, task)` (`api/flow/dispatch/taskRequirements.js`) derives `{ harness, model, tier, alias, runnableType, kind, stageId }`, then each candidate is tested with `runnerMatches(runner, req, { projectId })` (`api/flow/dispatch/runnerMatches.js`). Misses are loud reason codes aggregated into the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` response.\n14|3. **Runner-side operator settings** are enforced in one shared helper, `operatorConfigVerdict(record, req, opts)` (`api/flow/dispatch/operatorConfigVerdict.js`), called from **three** paths so no claim route bypasses it: the dispatch scan (via `runnerMatches`), direct claim (`POST /tasks/:id/claim`), and merge-lease acquire (`POST /runners/integration/acquire`). This is the pattern to copy.\n15|4. **Runner identity** is the `runnerId` string (`api/flow/runnerRegistry.js`). There are currently **no runner labels/tags** anywhere (no `labels` on records, facts, capabilities, or `operatorConfig`) — labels would be new infrastructure on both Runner (advertise) and backend (store/match) sides.\n16|5. **Fleet visibility**: `GET /runners`, `GET /runners/monitor`, scheduler deferral counters. Runner list UI consumes `frontend/src/services/runners/index.js`.\n17|\n18|Consequences for the design:\n19|\n20|- A **stage-centric** rule (stage names which runners may take it) is the mirror image of the existing **runner-centric** `operatorConfig` (runner names which projects/tools it serves). It needs its own verdict helper but the same \"one enforcement point, called from every claim path\" discipline.\n21|- The merge/integration path is the awkward one: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` currently enforces with only `{ kind: \"merge\" }` and no stage context. A stage allow/block list for merge stages requires resolving the task/stage there (or explicitly scoping merge out of v1 — see decision D6).\n22|\n23|## 2. Survey questions → decisions (all ASSUMPTION until SURVEY.md is answered)\n24|\n25|| # | Survey question | Recommended default | Why |\n26||---|---|---|---|\n27|| q1 | Names, labels, or both? | **Names (runnerId) in v1; labels as a named follow-up** | Names need no new Runner/backend infrastructure and cover both motivating examples (one publish runner, onsite-only scan — operators know their runnerIds). Labels require advertise/store/match plumbing on both sides; scoping them into v1 roughly doubles the work. Data model should reserve a shape labels can extend (see D1). |\n28|| q2 | One list + mode toggle, or two lists? | **One list + mode toggle** (`Only these runners` / `All except these runners`), empty/off = unrestricted | One list has no conflict semantics to define (two lists need an allow-vs-block precedence rule). Matches \"optional whitelist/blacklist\" in the task. |\n29|| q3 | No allowed runner available? | **Keep waiting + loud surfacing, never fail the task** | Matches existing `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` behavior; failing would destroy queued work. Loudness comes from a new miss reason (e.g. `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`) in dispatch misses + monitor/scheduler visibility. Manual override stays possible via existing operator moves (skip/reassign), not a new bypass. |\n30|| q4 | Stage-only, or also project/group defaults? | **Stage-only in v1** | The task asks for stage settings. Project defaults add precedence rules (stage vs project) for unclear value; note as follow-up. |\n31|| q5 | Who can edit the lists? | **Anyone who can already edit stage settings** | No new permission system exists for stages; inventing one is out of scope. |\n32|| q6 | Who sets runner labels? (conditional on labels) | **Admin-managed, centrally** (when labels land) | The compliance use case (\"must run onsite\") is meaningless if the runner self-declares its labels. Self-reported labels are spoofable; operator-owned labels in `operatorConfig` are enforceable. |\n33|\n34|If any survey answer comes back differently, the plan still holds; only the flagged decision (D1–D6 in §4) changes.\n35|\n36|## 3. Approach: make the change easy, then make the change\n37|\n38|**Make the change easy (Phases 0–2, behavior-preserving):**\n39|\n40|- P0: research spikes that retire the two unknowns (embedded execution path; merge-lease stage resolution) before any code is shaped around them.\n41|- P1: introduce the shared stage-runner-policy module + normalization + fixtures with **no wiring** (pure functions, fully tested, nothing calls them yet).\n42|- P2: plumb `stageId`/stage context to the one claim path that lacks it (integration acquire) in a behavior-preserving way, or formally scope merge out with a recorded decision.\n43|\n44|**Make the change (Phases 3–6):**\n45|\n46|- P3: data model + normalization + persistence round-trip.\n47|- P4: enforcement on all claim paths + loud miss reasons.\n48|- P5: UI at the bottom of stage settings.\n49|- P6: observability, docs, rollout/verification.\n50|\n51|## 4. Key design decisions\n52|\n53|- **D1 — Storage shape.** `stage.runnerPolicy = { mode: \"allow\" | \"block\", runners: [<runnerId>, …] }`, entirely optional/absent = unrestricted. Empty `runners` array = unrestricted regardless of mode (fail-open, never strand tasks on a mis-edit). Additive normalization in `normalizeStage` (trim, drop non-strings/blanks, dedupe) mirroring the `tags`/`skills` pattern. Reserve sibling key space (`labels` later) rather than overloading `runners`.\n54|- **D2 — Enforcement point.** New pure helper `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(stageOrPolicy, runnerId, …)` in `api/flow/dispatch/` (sibling to `operatorConfigVerdict.js`), returning `{ ok, reason }` with loud codes (`RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `RUNNER_BLOCKED`). `taskRequirements()` attaches the current stage's policy to `req` (it already returns `stageId`); `runnerMatches()` enforces it **after** health/capability gates (stable existing reasons keep priority — a stale runner still reports `STALE_HEARTBEAT`) and alongside `operatorConfigVerdict`. Direct claim and integration-acquire routes call the same helper so no path bypasses it.\n55|- **D3 — No fallback execution.** A restricted stage with no eligible runner waits (q3). No silent reroute to another runner, no auto-fail. The stuck/stall surfacing must distinguish \"waiting for an allowed runner\" from generic idleness (see P6).\n56|- **D4 — Name matching.** Exact `runnerId` match (trimmed). Unknown/deleted runnerIds in a policy are kept (not auto-pruned) and shown as stale in the UI, so renaming a runner doesn't silently open a compliance gate. Renames are an operator action with a visible warning.\n57|- **D5 — Human/non-runnable stages.** Policy is stored but inert for stages with `runnableType: false` (human stages never dispatch). UI may still show the control for consistency, with a note that it only affects runner-dispatched work.\n58|- **D6 — Merge stages in v1.** Preferred: enforce on the merge path too (publish/merge is a motivating example). Cost: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` must resolve task→stage to get the policy. If P0/P2 shows that resolution is invasive, fallback is: v1 covers agent/review/ci; merge enforcement is a fast follow with the same helper. Record whichever is chosen.\n59|\n60|## 5. Output: a short note stating which execution paths a stage policy must cover, or proof that claim-path enforcement is sufficient.\n65|- **0.2 Merge-path stage resolution spike.** Determine the cheapest correct way to get from `POST /runners/integration/acquire` (`projectId` + `targetBranch`) to the task's current stage policy. Options: (a) look up the queued task in `integrationQueue` by project+branch, (b) require `taskId` in the acquire body, (c) enforce only at dispatch time (the dispatch route already has `cand.stage`) and treat direct acquire as operator-trusted. Output: chosen option + estimate; feeds D6.\n66|- **0.3 Runner inventory UX check.** Confirm `GET /runners` returns everything the stage-settings picker needs (id + displayName + status). Non-goals (v1)\n147|\n148|- Runner labels/tags (q1 follow-up / Phase 7 candidate).\n149|- Project-level or stage-group defaults (q4 follow-up).\n150|- New edit permissions (q5: existing stage editors).\n151|- Manual override/bypass UI beyond existing operator moves (q3 default is wait + visibility).\n152|- Auto-pruning or auto-migrating policies on runner rename/delete (D4: show stale, keep closed).","isError":false,"source":"runner-structured","runnerId":"server2-alpha","executionId":"aa6de9b2-e724-4842-ae7d-4004fca1efac","seq":16},"fidelity":"normalized","seq":57,"blockId":"b32","tool":{"callId":"call_01a199cdbc4c70b49364327827322182","name":"read_file","args":"{\"path\":\"docs/tasks/a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md\"}","result":"Read text file `docs/tasks/a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.\n1|# Implementation Plan — Whitelist/Blacklist Runners on Stages\n2|\n3|- Task: `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\n4|- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take publish stages, or a compliance scan runs only on onsite runners — without blocking a whole category.\n5|- Principle: **make the change easy, then make the change.** Phases 0–2 are research and preparatory refactors that land as safe, behavior-preserving steps; Phases 3–6 are the actual feature.\n6|- Survey status: `SURVEY.md` exists but has **no answers** (`\"answers\": {}`). Section 2 records each survey question with a recommended default (marked ASSUMPTION) so implementation can proceed; if the survey is later answered differently, only the flagged decision points change.\n7|\n8|## 1. Current architecture (grounded)\n9|\n10|How a Runner gets work today:\n11|\n12|1. **Stages** live on the project/workflow (`project.stages`, persisted in `project.json`). Shape is normalized additively in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js` (`normalizeStage` / `normalizeStages`): new optional fields are tolerated only when present so legacy stages round-trip byte-identically. Stage editor UI is `frontend/src/components/workflow/StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (+ `StageTypeConfig.jsx`); the pause/fallback section at the bottom is the natural neighbor for the new control.\n13|2. **Dispatch scan** (`POST /runners/dispatch` in `api/flow/runnerRoutes.js`): `scanClaimableTasks()` (`api/flow/dispatch/scanClaimableTasks.js`) finds candidate tasks, `taskRequirements(project, task)` (`api/flow/dispatch/taskRequirements.js`) derives `{ harness, model, tier, alias, runnableType, kind, stageId }`, then each candidate is tested with `runnerMatches(runner, req, { projectId })` (`api/flow/dispatch/runnerMatches.js`). Misses are loud reason codes aggregated into the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` response.\n14|3. **Runner-side operator settings** are enforced in one shared helper, `operatorConfigVerdict(record, req, opts)` (`api/flow/dispatch/operatorConfigVerdict.js`), called from **three** paths so no claim route bypasses it: the dispatch scan (via `runnerMatches`), direct claim (`POST /tasks/:id/claim`), and merge-lease acquire (`POST /runners/integration/acquire`). This is the pattern to copy.\n15|4. **Runner identity** is the `runnerId` string (`api/flow/runnerRegistry.js`). There are currently **no runner labels/tags** anywhere (no `labels` on records, facts, capabilities, or `operatorConfig`) — labels would be new infrastructure on both Runner (advertise) and backend (store/match) sides.\n16|5. **Fleet visibility**: `GET /runners`, `GET /runners/monitor`, scheduler deferral counters. Runner list UI consumes `frontend/src/services/runners/index.js`.\n17|\n18|Consequences for the design:\n19|\n20|- A **stage-centric** rule (stage names which runners may take it) is the mirror image of the existing **runner-centric** `operatorConfig` (runner names which projects/tools it serves). It needs its own verdict helper but the same \"one enforcement point, called from every claim path\" discipline.\n21|- The merge/integration path is the awkward one: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` currently enforces with only `{ kind: \"merge\" }` and no stage context. A stage allow/block list for merge stages requires resolving the task/stage there (or explicitly scoping merge out of v1 — see decision D6).\n22|\n23|## 2. Survey questions → decisions (all ASSUMPTION until SURVEY.md is answered)\n24|\n25|| # | Survey question | Recommended default | Why |\n26||---|---|---|---|\n27|| q1 | Names, labels, or both? | **Names (runnerId) in v1; labels as a named follow-up** | Names need no new Runner/backend infrastructure and cover both motivating examples (one publish runner, onsite-only scan — operators know their runnerIds). Labels require advertise/store/match plumbing on both sides; scoping them into v1 roughly doubles the work. Data model should reserve a shape labels can extend (see D1). |\n28|| q2 | One list + mode toggle, or two lists? | **One list + mode toggle** (`Only these runners` / `All except these runners`), empty/off = unrestricted | One list has no conflict semantics to define (two lists need an allow-vs-block precedence rule). Matches \"optional whitelist/blacklist\" in the task. |\n29|| q3 | No allowed runner available? | **Keep waiting + loud surfacing, never fail the task** | Matches existing `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` behavior; failing would destroy queued work. Loudness comes from a new miss reason (e.g. `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`) in dispatch misses + monitor/scheduler visibility. Manual override stays possible via existing operator moves (skip/reassign), not a new bypass. |\n30|| q4 | Stage-only, or also project/group defaults? | **Stage-only in v1** | The task asks for stage settings. Project defaults add precedence rules (stage vs project) for unclear value; note as follow-up. |\n31|| q5 | Who can edit the lists? | **Anyone who can already edit stage settings** | No new permission system exists for stages; inventing one is out of scope. |\n32|| q6 | Who sets runner labels? (conditional on labels) | **Admin-managed, centrally** (when labels land) | The compliance use case (\"must run onsite\") is meaningless if the runner self-declares its labels. Self-reported labels are spoofable; operator-owned labels in `operatorConfig` are enforceable. |\n33|\n34|If any survey answer comes back differently, the plan still holds; only the flagged decision (D1–D6 in §4) changes.\n35|\n36|## 3. Approach: make the change easy, then make the change\n37|\n38|**Make the change easy (Phases 0–2, behavior-preserving):**\n39|\n40|- P0: research spikes that retire the two unknowns (embedded execution path; merge-lease stage resolution) before any code is shaped around them.\n41|- P1: introduce the shared stage-runner-policy module + normalization + fixtures with **no wiring** (pure functions, fully tested, nothing calls them yet).\n42|- P2: plumb `stageId`/stage context to the one claim path that lacks it (integration acquire) in a behavior-preserving way, or formally scope merge out with a recorded decision.\n43|\n44|**Make the change (Phases 3–6):**\n45|\n46|- P3: data model + normalization + persistence round-trip.\n47|- P4: enforcement on all claim paths + loud miss reasons.\n48|- P5: UI at the bottom of stage settings.\n49|- P6: observability, docs, rollout/verification.\n50|\n51|## 4. Key design decisions\n52|\n53|- **D1 — Storage shape.** `stage.runnerPolicy = { mode: \"allow\" | \"block\", runners: [<runnerId>, …] }`, entirely optional/absent = unrestricted. Empty `runners` array = unrestricted regardless of mode (fail-open, never strand tasks on a mis-edit). Additive normalization in `normalizeStage` (trim, drop non-strings/blanks, dedupe) mirroring the `tags`/`skills` pattern. Reserve sibling key space (`labels` later) rather than overloading `runners`.\n54|- **D2 — Enforcement point.** New pure helper `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(stageOrPolicy, runnerId, …)` in `api/flow/dispatch/` (sibling to `operatorConfigVerdict.js`), returning `{ ok, reason }` with loud codes (`RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `RUNNER_BLOCKED`). `taskRequirements()` attaches the current stage's policy to `req` (it already returns `stageId`); `runnerMatches()` enforces it **after** health/capability gates (stable existing reasons keep priority — a stale runner still reports `STALE_HEARTBEAT`) and alongside `operatorConfigVerdict`. Direct claim and integration-acquire routes call the same helper so no path bypasses it.\n55|- **D3 — No fallback execution.** A restricted stage with no eligible runner waits (q3). No silent reroute to another runner, no auto-fail. The stuck/stall surfacing must distinguish \"waiting for an allowed runner\" from generic idleness (see P6).\n56|- **D4 — Name matching.** Exact `runnerId` match (trimmed). Unknown/deleted runnerIds in a policy are kept (not auto-pruned) and shown as stale in the UI, so renaming a runner doesn't silently open a compliance gate. Renames are an operator action with a visible warning.\n57|- **D5 — Human/non-runnable stages.** Policy is stored but inert for stages with `runnableType: false` (human stages never dispatch). UI may still show the control for consistency, with a note that it only affects runner-dispatched work.\n58|- **D6 — Merge stages in v1.** Preferred: enforce on the merge path too (publish/merge is a motivating example). Cost: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` must resolve task→stage to get the policy. If P0/P2 shows that resolution is invasive, fallback is: v1 covers agent/review/ci; merge enforcement is a fast follow with the same helper. Record whichever is chosen.\n59|\n60|## 5. Work plan (ordered; big/research pieces first)\n61|\n62|### Phase 0 — Research spikes (no production code; timeboxed)\n63|\n64|- **0.1 Embedded execution audit.** Confirm whether any stage type still executes on the backend without a Runner claim (notes say every command stage is Runner-runnable since DA9 retirement; verify for agent/review via `executeStage.js` / scheduler paths). Output: a short note stating which execution paths a stage policy must cover, or proof that claim-path enforcement is sufficient.\n65|- **0.2 Merge-path stage resolution spike.** Determine the cheapest correct way to get from `POST /runners/integration/acquire` (`projectId` + `targetBranch`) to the task's current stage policy. Options: (a) look up the queued task in `integrationQueue` by project+branch, (b) require `taskId` in the acquire body, (c) enforce only at dispatch time (the dispatch route already has `cand.stage`) and treat direct acquire as operator-trusted. Output: chosen option + estimate; feeds D6.\n66|- **0.3 Runner inventory UX check.** Confirm `GET /runners` returns everything the stage-settings picker needs (id + displayName + status). If display names are missing for some records, the picker falls back to runnerId — no backend change.\n67|\n68|### Phase 1 — Make the change easy: shared policy module (no wiring)\n69|\n70|- **1.1** Create `api/flow/dispatch/stageRunnerPolicy.js` (pure): `normalizeStageRunnerPolicy(raw)` + `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(policy, runnerId)`. Fail-open on absent/malformed input (legacy-safe, like `operatorConfigVerdict`). Full unit tests, including: absent policy → allow; empty list → allow; allow-mode hit/miss; block-mode hit/miss; malformed shapes → allow; whitespace/dupes normalized.\n71|- **1.2** Shared test fixtures: a small factory for stage objects with policies + runner records, reusable by dispatch/route tests. (Check existing `test-helpers` first; extend, don't duplicate.)\n72|- **1.3** Wire **nothing** yet. This phase lands as dead-but-tested code, reviewable in isolation.\n73|\n74|### Phase 2 — Make the change easy: context plumbing (behavior-preserving)\n75|\n76|- **2.1** `taskRequirements()` attaches the stage's normalized policy to the returned `req` (e.g. `req.runnerPolicy`) alongside the existing `stageId`. No consumer reads it yet → no behavior change. Update `taskRequirements` characterization tests to assert the new field.\n77|- **2.2** Per P0.2 outcome: plumb stage context into the integration-acquire path (still unenforced), or record the D6 fallback. No behavior change.\n78|- **2.3** Add the new miss-reason strings to whatever centralizes reason codes / monitor copy (if such a registry exists; otherwise co-locate with the verdict helper). No behavior change.\n79|\n80|### Phase 3 — Data model + normalization + persistence\n81|\n82|- **3.1** `normalizeStage()` in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js`: additive `runnerPolicy` handling (present-only, legacy round-trip identical). Unit tests: legacy stages untouched; valid policies preserved; junk coerced per D1.\n83|- **3.2** Persistence round-trip: save/load a project + workflow carrying policies (`projectStore` tests), including multi-workflow (`createWorkflow`/`updateWorkflow` paths that call `normalizeStages`).\n84|- **3.3** Validation at the API boundary: stage create/update routes reject unknown `mode` values with 400 (but never reject unknown runnerIds — D4).\n85|\n86|### Phase 4 — Enforcement on every claim path\n87|\n88|- **4.1** `runnerMatches()`: enforce `req.runnerPolicy` after capability gates, before/after `operatorConfigVerdict` (order: keep operator verdict last so runner-owner settings stay the final word, or first — pick one, document it, test the combined case). New loud reasons in the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` misses map.\n89|- **4.2** `POST /tasks/:id/claim`: call the verdict helper with the task's current stage policy; refuse with 409 + reason code (mirrors the existing `operatorConfigVerdict` 409 block in that route).\n90|- **4.3** Merge path per D6: `POST /runners/dispatch` merge branch (has `cand.stage` — straightforward) and `POST /runners/integration/acquire` (uses P2.2 plumbing).\n91|- **4.4** `selectRunnerForTask()` inherits enforcement via `runnerMatches` — add a test proving it.\n92|- **4.5** Negative tests: a blocked runner polling dispatch gets `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` with the new reason (not a task); direct claim by a non-allowed runner gets 409; unrestricted stages behave exactly as before (regression sweep of existing dispatch/claim tests).\n93|\n94|### Phase 5 — UI: bottom of stage settings\n95|\n96|- **5.1** New section at the bottom of `StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (below pause/fallback): mode selector (`Off` / `Only these runners` / `All except these runners`) + runner multi-select fed by `GET /runners` (displayName with runnerId fallback; status shown; stale/unknown ids preserved and badged). Reuse `SelectDropdown` / existing multi-select patterns; follow `StageTagEditor` as the closest \"list editor in this drawer\" precedent.\n97|- **5.2** Save path: `onUpdateStage(stage.id, { runnerPolicy })` round-trips through the existing stage save; \"Off\"/empty clears to absent (not an empty object) so stored stages stay clean.\n98|- **5.3** Frontend tests: renders section; mode + selection persist via `onUpdateStage`; stale-id badging; empty/off clears. Headless only (see §6); no long-lived dev servers.\n99|- **5.4** Copy: one-line explanation per mode + a note that empty = all runners, and (per D5) that human stages are unaffected.\n100|\n101|### Phase 6 — Observability, docs, rollout\n102|\n103|- **6.1 Observability.** New miss reasons appear in dispatch `misses`, `GET /runners/monitor` (or equivalent), and any scheduler deferral diagnostics, so \"waiting for an allowed runner\" is distinguishable from \"no runners at all\". Add/extend a monitor widget test if the dashboard renders per-reason breakdowns.\n104|- **6.2 Docs.** Update: (a) user docs for stage settings (new section: what it does, examples for publish + onsite-compliance), (b) runner/operator docs (interaction with runner-side `operatorConfig` scoping — both gates apply), (c) `CHANGELOG.md` entry, (d) JSDoc on new/changed functions per repo convention. Check `site-docs` vs `docs` ownership before writing.\n105|- **6.3 Rollout.** Feature is additive and default-off: no migration, no flag needed. Rollout = deploy + verify unrestricted behavior unchanged + one restricted stage end-to-end (see §6). Rollback = deploy previous build; stored policies are inert to old code (unknown field ignored) — verify that claim with an old-code read test if cheap.\n106|\n107|## 6. Validation plan (how we prove it works)\n108|\n109|### Automated (primary gate: `npm run test:unit`)\n110|\n111|- **Unit:** policy normalize + verdict matrix (P1.1); `normalizeStage` legacy round-trip + coercion (P3.1); `taskRequirements` carries policy (P2.1); `runnerMatches` reason priority incl. combined operator+stage gates (P4.1); `selectRunnerForTask` inheritance (P4.4).\n112|- **Route/integration:** dispatch honors allow (only listed runner gets the task) and block (listed runner never does) across agent + ci stages; direct-claim 409s for refused runners; merge-path behavior per D6; `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` misses include the new codes with counts; full existing dispatch/claim/runner test files pass unmodified (regression proof that unrestricted behavior is identical).\n113|- **Frontend:** drawer section tests (P5.3) via the existing headless runner; persistence round-trip through `saveStages` mocks.\n114|- **Golden/characterization:** a project.json with policies survives save→load→save byte-stable (apart from intended normalization).\n115|\n116|### Manual / headless checks (no long-lived servers)\n117|\n118|- Two-runner scenario (or fixtures/mocks): restrict a CI \"publish\" stage to runner A; poll dispatch as B → `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` with `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`/`RUNNER_BLOCKED`; poll as A → assigned. Same for block mode inverted.\n119|- Stage-settings UI check via the headless screenshot tool (`npm run capture:screenshot`) if visual confirmation is wanted — no `npm run dev` background servers.\n120|- Monitor/scheduler surfaces show the waiting-for-allowed-runner state distinctly.\n121|\n122|### Documentation proof\n123|\n124|- Docs review checklist: stage-settings doc, runner doc, changelog entry, JSDoc examples all present and linked; `npm run docs:check`-style link checks pass if applicable.\n125|\n126|## 7. Risks and mitigations\n127|\n128|- **Stranded tasks (policy names a dead/renamed runner).** Mitigated by D3 (wait, don't fail) + D4 (stale ids visible, never auto-pruned) + observability (P6.1). No silent opening of gates.\n129|- **Bypass via an unpatched claim path.** Mitigated by the single-helper discipline (D2) + a negative test per path (P4.5) + P0.1 audit.\n130|- **Labels expected but not delivered.** q1 follow-up is explicit: v1 = names. If compliance reviewers insist on labels, that is a scoped Phase 7 (runner label advertise/store/match + admin UI), estimated separately — the `runnerPolicy` shape reserves room for it.\n131|- **Scope creep (project defaults, permissions).** q4/q5 defaults recorded; anything beyond stage-only + existing editors is a follow-up.\n132|- **Survey answered differently.** Only D-decisions change; phases stay the same shape. Re-check §2 before P3.\n133|\n134|## 8. Open questions\n135|\n136|- None blocking: every survey question has a recommended default in §2. The two genuine unknowns are retired by timeboxed spikes (P0.1, P0.2) before any production code is shaped around them.\n137|\n138|## 9. Success criteria\n139|\n140|- An operator can set, save, and clear a per-stage runner allow/block list from the bottom of stage settings.\n141|- Restricted stages dispatch/claim **only** to eligible runners on **every** claim path; ineligible runners get loud, specific refusals.\n142|- Unrestricted stages (including all legacy stages) behave exactly as before — proven by the existing suite passing unmodified plus new regression tests.\n143|- \"Waiting for an allowed runner\" is visible in dispatch misses and monitor surfaces, distinct from fleet-down states.\n144|- Docs + changelog updated; `npm run test:unit` green.\n145|\n146|## 10. Same for block mode inverted.\n119|- Stage-settings UI check via the headless screenshot tool (`npm run capture:screenshot`) if visual confirmation is wanted — no `npm run dev` background servers.\n120|- Monitor/scheduler surfaces show the waiting-for-allowed-runner state distinctly.\n121|\n122|### Documentation proof\n123|\n124|- Docs review checklist: stage-settings doc, runner doc, changelog entry, JSDoc examples all present and linked; `npm run docs:check`-style link checks pass if applicable.\n125|\n126|## 7. Risks and mitigations\n127|\n128|- **Stranded tasks (policy names a dead/renamed runner).** Mitigated by D3 (wait, don't fail) + D4 (stale ids visible, never auto-pruned) + observability (P6.1). No silent opening of gates.\n129|- **Bypass via an unpatched claim path.** Mitigated by the single-helper discipline (D2) + a negative test per path (P4.5) + P0.1 audit.\n130|- **Labels expected but not delivered.** q1 follow-up is explicit: v1 = names. If compliance reviewers insist on labels, that is a scoped Phase 7 (runner label advertise/store/match + admin UI), estimated separately — the `runnerPolicy` shape reserves room for it.\n131|- **Scope creep (project defaults, permissions).** q4/q5 defaults recorded; anything beyond stage-only + existing editors is a follow-up.\n132|- **Survey answered differently.** Only D-decisions change; phases stay the same shape. Re-check §2 before P3.\n133|\n134|## 8. Open questions\n135|\n136|- None blocking: every survey question has a recommended default in §2. The two genuine unknowns are retired by timeboxed spikes (P0.1, P0.2) before any production code is shaped around them.\n137|\n138|## 9. Success criteria\n139|\n140|- An operator can set, save, and clear a per-stage runner allow/block list from the bottom of stage settings.\n141|- Restricted stages dispatch/claim **only** to eligible runners on **every** claim path; ineligible runners get loud, specific refusals.\n142|- Unrestricted stages (including all legacy stages) behave exactly as before — proven by the existing suite passing unmodified plus new regression tests.\n143|- \"Waiting for an allowed runner\" is visible in dispatch misses and monitor surfaces, distinct from fleet-down states.\n144|- Docs + changelog updated; `npm run test:unit` green.\n145|\n146|## 10. text file `docs/tasks/a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.\n1|# Implementation Plan — Whitelist/Blacklist Runners on Stages\n2|\n3|- Task: `a3753643-7aa5-49da-8201-4499615200b6`\n4|- Goal: at the bottom of stage settings, an **optional** runner allow/block rule per stage, so (e.g.) only one runner can take publish stages, or a compliance scan runs only on onsite runners — without blocking a whole category.\n5|- Principle: **make the change easy, then make the change.** Phases 0–2 are research and preparatory refactors that land as safe, behavior-preserving steps; Phases 3–6 are the actual feature.\n6|- Survey status: `SURVEY.md` exists but has **no answers** (`\"answers\": {}`). Section 2 records each survey question with a recommended default (marked ASSUMPTION) so implementation can proceed; if the survey is later answered differently, only the flagged decision points change.\n7|\n8|## 1. Current architecture (grounded)\n9|\n10|How a Runner gets work today:\n11|\n12|1. **Stages** live on the project/workflow (`project.stages`, persisted in `project.json`). Shape is normalized additively in `api/project/stageNormalizers.js` (`normalizeStage` / `normalizeStages`): new optional fields are tolerated only when present so legacy stages round-trip byte-identically. Stage editor UI is `frontend/src/components/workflow/StageSettingsDrawer.jsx` (+ `StageTypeConfig.jsx`); the pause/fallback section at the bottom is the natural neighbor for the new control.\n13|2. **Dispatch scan** (`POST /runners/dispatch` in `api/flow/runnerRoutes.js`): `scanClaimableTasks()` (`api/flow/dispatch/scanClaimableTasks.js`) finds candidate tasks, `taskRequirements(project, task)` (`api/flow/dispatch/taskRequirements.js`) derives `{ harness, model, tier, alias, runnableType, kind, stageId }`, then each candidate is tested with `runnerMatches(runner, req, { projectId })` (`api/flow/dispatch/runnerMatches.js`). Misses are loud reason codes aggregated into the `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` response.\n14|3. **Runner-side operator settings** are enforced in one shared helper, `operatorConfigVerdict(record, req, opts)` (`api/flow/dispatch/operatorConfigVerdict.js`), called from **three** paths so no claim route bypasses it: the dispatch scan (via `runnerMatches`), direct claim (`POST /tasks/:id/claim`), and merge-lease acquire (`POST /runners/integration/acquire`). This is the pattern to copy.\n15|4. **Runner identity** is the `runnerId` string (`api/flow/runnerRegistry.js`). There are currently **no runner labels/tags** anywhere (no `labels` on records, facts, capabilities, or `operatorConfig`) — labels would be new infrastructure on both Runner (advertise) and backend (store/match) sides.\n16|5. **Fleet visibility**: `GET /runners`, `GET /runners/monitor`, scheduler deferral counters. Runner list UI consumes `frontend/src/services/runners/index.js`.\n17|\n18|Consequences for the design:\n19|\n20|- A **stage-centric** rule (stage names which runners may take it) is the mirror image of the existing **runner-centric** `operatorConfig` (runner names which projects/tools it serves). It needs its own verdict helper but the same \"one enforcement point, called from every claim path\" discipline.\n21|- The merge/integration path is the awkward one: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` currently enforces with only `{ kind: \"merge\" }` and no stage context. A stage allow/block list for merge stages requires resolving the task/stage there (or explicitly scoping merge out of v1 — see decision D6).\n22|\n23|## 2. Survey questions → decisions (all ASSUMPTION until SURVEY.md is answered)\n24|\n25|| # | Survey question | Recommended default | Why |\n26||---|---|---|---|\n27|| q1 | Names, labels, or both? | **Names (runnerId) in v1; labels as a named follow-up** | Names need no new Runner/backend infrastructure and cover both motivating examples (one publish runner, onsite-only scan — operators know their runnerIds). Labels require advertise/store/match plumbing on both sides; scoping them into v1 roughly doubles the work. Data model should reserve a shape labels can extend (see D1). |\n28|| q2 | One list + mode toggle, or two lists? | **One list + mode toggle** (`Only these runners` / `All except these runners`), empty/off = unrestricted | One list has no conflict semantics to define (two lists need an allow-vs-block precedence rule). Matches \"optional whitelist/blacklist\" in the task. |\n29|| q3 | No allowed runner available? | **Keep waiting + loud surfacing, never fail the task** | Matches existing `NO_CAPABLE_MATCH` behavior; failing would destroy queued work. Loudness comes from a new miss reason (e.g. `RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`) in dispatch misses + monitor/scheduler visibility. Manual override stays possible via existing operator moves (skip/reassign), not a new bypass. |\n30|| q4 | Stage-only, or also project/group defaults? | **Stage-only in v1** | The task asks for stage settings. Project defaults add precedence rules (stage vs project) for unclear value; note as follow-up. |\n31|| q5 | Who can edit the lists? | **Anyone who can already edit stage settings** | No new permission system exists for stages; inventing one is out of scope. |\n32|| q6 | Who sets runner labels? (conditional on labels) | **Admin-managed, centrally** (when labels land) | The compliance use case (\"must run onsite\") is meaningless if the runner self-declares its labels. Self-reported labels are spoofable; operator-owned labels in `operatorConfig` are enforceable. |\n33|\n34|If any survey answer comes back differently, the plan still holds; only the flagged decision (D1–D6 in §4) changes.\n35|\n36|## 3. Approach: make the change easy, then make the change\n37|\n38|**Make the change easy (Phases 0–2, behavior-preserving):**\n39|\n40|- P0: research spikes that retire the two unknowns (embedded execution path; merge-lease stage resolution) before any code is shaped around them.\n41|- P1: introduce the shared stage-runner-policy module + normalization + fixtures with **no wiring** (pure functions, fully tested, nothing calls them yet).\n42|- P2: plumb `stageId`/stage context to the one claim path that lacks it (integration acquire) in a behavior-preserving way, or formally scope merge out with a recorded decision.\n43|\n44|**Make the change (Phases 3–6):**\n45|\n46|- P3: data model + normalization + persistence round-trip.\n47|- P4: enforcement on all claim paths + loud miss reasons.\n48|- P5: UI at the bottom of stage settings.\n49|- P6: observability, docs, rollout/verification.\n50|\n51|## 4. Key design decisions\n52|\n53|- **D1 — Storage shape.** `stage.runnerPolicy = { mode: \"allow\" | \"block\", runners: [<runnerId>, …] }`, entirely optional/absent = unrestricted. Empty `runners` array = unrestricted regardless of mode (fail-open, never strand tasks on a mis-edit). Additive normalization in `normalizeStage` (trim, drop non-strings/blanks, dedupe) mirroring the `tags`/`skills` pattern. Reserve sibling key space (`labels` later) rather than overloading `runners`.\n54|- **D2 — Enforcement point.** New pure helper `stageRunnerPolicyVerdict(stageOrPolicy, runnerId, …)` in `api/flow/dispatch/` (sibling to `operatorConfigVerdict.js`), returning `{ ok, reason }` with loud codes (`RUNNER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `RUNNER_BLOCKED`). `taskRequirements()` attaches the current stage's policy to `req` (it already returns `stageId`); `runnerMatches()` enforces it **after** health/capability gates (stable existing reasons keep priority — a stale runner still reports `STALE_HEARTBEAT`) and alongside `operatorConfigVerdict`. Direct claim and integration-acquire routes call the same helper so no path bypasses it.\n55|- **D3 — No fallback execution.** A restricted stage with no eligible runner waits (q3). No silent reroute to another runner, no auto-fail. The stuck/stall surfacing must distinguish \"waiting for an allowed runner\" from generic idleness (see P6).\n56|- **D4 — Name matching.** Exact `runnerId` match (trimmed). Unknown/deleted runnerIds in a policy are kept (not auto-pruned) and shown as stale in the UI, so renaming a runner doesn't silently open a compliance gate. Renames are an operator action with a visible warning.\n57|- **D5 — Human/non-runnable stages.** Policy is stored but inert for stages with `runnableType: false` (human stages never dispatch). UI may still show the control for consistency, with a note that it only affects runner-dispatched work.\n58|- **D6 — Merge stages in v1.** Preferred: enforce on the merge path too (publish/merge is a motivating example). Cost: `POST /runners/integration/acquire` must resolve task→stage to get the policy. If P0/P2 shows that resolution is invasive, fallback is: v1 covers agent/review/ci; merge enforcement is a fast follow with the same helper. Record whichever is chosen.\n59|\n60|## 5. Same for block mode inverted.\n119|- Stage-settings UI check via the headless screenshot tool (`npm run capture:screenshot`) if visual confirmation is wanted — no `npm run dev` background servers.\n120|- Monitor/scheduler surfaces show the waiting-for-allowed-runner state distinctly.\n121|\n122|### Documentation proof\n123|\n124|- Docs review checklist: stage-settings doc, runner doc, changelog entry, JSDoc examples all present and linked; `npm run docs:check`-style link checks pass if applicable.\n125|\n126|## 7. Risks and mitigations\n127|\n128|- **Stranded tasks (policy names a dead/renamed runner).** Mitigated by D3 (wait, don't fail) + D4 (stale ids visible, never auto-pruned) + observability (P6.1). No silent opening of gates.\n129|- **Bypass via an unpatched claim path.** Mitigated by the single-helper discipline (D2) + a negative test per path (P4.5) + P0.1 audit.\n130|- **Labels expected but not delivered.** q1 follow-up is explicit: v1 = names. If compliance reviewers insist on labels, that is a scoped Phase 7 (runner label advertise/store/match + admin UI), estimated separately — the `runnerPolicy` shape reserves room for it.\n131|- **Scope creep (project defaults, permissions).** q4/q5 defaults recorded; anything beyond stage-only + existing editors is a follow-up.\n132|- **Survey answered differently.** Only D-decisions change; phases stay the same shape. Re-check §2 before P3.\n133|\n134|## 8. Open questions\n135|\n136|- None blocking: every survey question has a recommended default in §2. The two genuine unknowns are retired by timeboxed spikes (P0.1, P0.2) before any production code is shaped around them.\n137|\n138|## 9. Success criteria\n139|\n140|- An operator can set, save, and clear a per-stage runner allow/block list from the bottom of stage settings.\n141|- Restricted stages dispatch/claim **only** to eligible runners on **every** claim path; ineligible runners get loud, specific refusals.\n142|- Unrestricted stages (including all legacy stages) behave exactly as before — proven by the existing suite passing unmodified plus new regression tests.\n143|- \"Waiting for an allowed runner\" is visible in dispatch misses and monitor surfaces, distinct from fleet-down states.\n144|- Docs + changelog updated; `npm run test:unit` green.\n145|\n146|## 10. Non-goals (v1)\n147|\n148|- Runner labels/tags (q1 follow-up / Phase 7 candidate).\n149|- Project-level or stage-group defaults (q4 follow-up).\n150|- New edit permissions (q5: existing stage editors).\n151|- Manual override/bypass UI beyond existing operator moves (q3 default is wait + visibility).\n152|- Auto-pruning or auto-migrating policies on runner rename/delete (D4: show stale, keep closed)."}},{"id":59,"ts":"2026-11-02T01:09:24.026Z","feed":"main","feeds":["main","tools"],"kind":"tool","turn":1,"text":"[TOOL] edit_file({\"find\":\"fallback is: v1 covers agent/review/ci;
